# ODBC Crusher — Improvement Plan

**Created**: 2026-09-07
**Supersedes**: [old-IMPROVEMENT_PLAN.md](./old-IMPROVEMENT_PLAN.md) (the 2026-04-24 → 2026-04-27 plan, §1–§8; a verbatim copy remains in the gitignored `tmp/old-IMPROVEMENT_PLAN_INITIAL.md`)
**Baseline commit**: `17d6182`
**Scope**: full-codebase review — application (`src/`), mock driver (`mock-driver/`), test architecture (`tests/`), build system, CI/CD, CLI/output contract, documentation.

---

## How to keep this plan updated

This document is the single work queue for the project. **Update it in the same commit as the change it describes** — never in a follow-up commit, or it drifts.

**Every time you finish a task:**

1. Set **Status** to `✅ DONE` (only once the fix is implemented *and* tested — see below).
2. Fill the **Commit** column with the short hash (`git rev-parse --short HEAD`).
3. Rewrite the **Details** cell to say what was *actually* done, not what was planned. If the implementation diverged, say so and why.
4. If the work uncovered new problems, add new rows rather than widening an existing task.

**Status values** — use exactly these:

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ DONE | Fix implemented **and** tested. Not DONE until a test would catch its regression, or Details explains why no test is possible. |
| 🔧 IN PROGRESS | Partially implemented or underway. Note in Details what is left. |
| ❌ OPEN | Not yet addressed. |
| ⏯️ DEFERRED | On hold until another task finishes. Name the blocking task ID in Details. |

**Rules that keep the plan honest** — the previous plan broke each of these at least once:

- **Never mark a task DONE on the strength of a plan entry. Re-read the code.** Prior §2.1 claimed "migrated all 23 test files" while `param_binding_tests.cpp` was 3-of-26 migrated. Prior §3.2/§3.6 claimed "every public entry point takes `HandleLock`" while `SQLFreeStmt` takes none.
- **Marking a task DONE and then re-loosening it elsewhere is not DONE.** Prior §7.10 stayed ✅ while §8 immediately relaxed the very assertion it had tightened.
- **"Implicitly addressed by another task" is not a resolution.** Prior §2.8 was closed that way and is untouched in the code.
- **Never delete a row.** If a task turns out to be wrong, keep it and change Details to explain the reversal.
- **Keep `README.md` counts in sync** whenever probe counts, pass rates, or the driver matrix change (H1–H3).
- **This file now lives in the tracked `docs/` directory** (**H8**, done). `tmp/` remains gitignored and is for scratch artifacts, clones and triage output only — never for living documents.

**Suggested order**: A (result integrity) → D (mock driver) → B (taxonomy) → E (build) → C (duplication) → F/G/H.

### If you only do ten things

Ordered by value per hour, not by section. Each is independently shippable.

| # | Task | Why first |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **D21** — add `tests/test_connection.cpp` to the mock build | 28 tests, 443 lines, never compiled in the project's history. One line of CMake. |
| 2 | **E1** + **D16** — apply the existing warning interface to all 8 targets | The flags already exist and reach 3 targets. Turning them on surfaces 9 real warnings, including an `int → SQLUSMALLINT` narrowing in a `SQLGetInfo` return path. `-Wshadow` alone catches **D14**/**D22**. |
| 3 | **A2** + **C2** — one dialect-fallback helper in `TestBase` | 58 bare `SELECT`s currently make the entire Escape Sequence category, all of Numeric Struct and all of Cursor Stress unusable against Firebird — the driver this repo sits inside. |
| 4 | **G1** — send progress output to stderr | The `-o json \| jq` usage the README documents does not work today. Two lines. |
| 5 | **A9** — catch `std::exception` in `run_test` | One uncaught exception currently discards all 195 results and writes no JSON, and the crash guard blames the driver for it. |
| 6 | **D23** + **D24** — bound the `SQLGetInfoW` memcpy and post `01004` on truncation | A real buffer overflow on the only `SQLGetInfo` path Windows exposes, plus the most likely cause of the §8.2 Unicode probe failures. |
| 7 | **A1** — stop turning a wrong value into `SKIP_INCONCLUSIVE` | The largest false-negative class in the suite; SKIP does not affect the exit code. |
| 8 | **D3** — bring `register.sh` to parity with `register.ps1` | One template edit; the single input that differs between the passing Windows CI slot and the failing Linux one. |
| 9 | **B1** + **B2** — fix or reclassify the ≥26 always-PASS probes | They put a fixed ~13% floor under every driver's advertised score. |
| 10 | **H1**–**H5**, **H8** — documentation truth pass | The README claims 191/195 (it is 195/195) and that Firebird is not in the matrix (it is); there is no LICENSE file; and this plan lives in a gitignored directory the project's own PROJECT_PLAN points at. |

## How these findings were verified

Everything below was checked against the running system, not read off the previous plan.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Clean rebuild of both projects, MSVC Debug | 0 errors, 0 warnings **as currently configured** |
| `ctest -L unit` | 27/27 pass (7 skip — no real driver configured) |
| `ctest -L e2e` | 14/14 pass |
| mock-driver `ctest` | 107/107 pass |
| `odbc-crusher` vs `Mode=Success` mock (Windows) | **195/195 PASS**, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 141 ms |
| Rebuild of **every** target with `/W4 /EHsc` | **3** new warnings in the app, **6** in the mock (E1) |
| Latest master CI run `25025987882` | all 14 jobs green |
| `-o json` piped into a JSON parser | **fails to parse** (G1) |
| Bare `SELECT <expr>` statements with no `FROM` fallback | **58**, across 4 probe files (A2) |
| ANSI entry points exported on Linux but not Windows | **38** (D2) |
| Mock test files on disk vs in the build | 10 vs **9** — `test_connection.cpp` (28 tests) never added (D21) |
| Probes that PASS on every branch | **≥26** of 195 (B1) |

The Windows run reproduces 195/195 against the mock. The Linux gap (6 FAIL / 23 SKIP, hard-coded as a tolerance in the e2e canary) was **not** reproducible here — no Linux toolchain on this host — so Linux-specific items rest on code reading plus the CI baseline constants.

## Part 0 — Corrections to the previous plan

Its Executive Summary claims that "every task in §1–§5 is now ✅ RESOLVED, ⏯️ DEFERRED with explicit gating, or 🚫 WONTFIX" and that its three headline weaknesses "are all closed". Both are too strong.

| Prior item | Prior status | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 Extract `run_test`; "migrated all 23 `src/tests/*.cpp` files" | ✅ RESOLVED | **WRONG** | `param_binding_tests.cpp` defines 26 `TestResult` functions; **3** call `run_test`. It retains **14** `make_result` sites and **28** hand-rolled `high_resolution_clock` sites. Repo-wide: 174 `run_test` vs 15 `make_result` + 36 manual-timing. The plan's own §6.5 says "14 of 25 unmigrated", contradicting §2.1's headline. The other 22 files *are* fully migrated. → **C1** |
| §2.8 Collect failing query text in retry loops | ✅ RESOLVED ("implicitly addressed by §2.1") | **WRONG** | `run_test` centralises the *outer* catch. The ~33 dialect-fallback loops each carry their own **inner** `catch (const OdbcError&) { continue; }` discarding query, SQLSTATE and message (`datatype_tests.cpp:57-59`). Nothing in §2.1 touches them. → **C2** |
| §3.2 / §3.6 "Every public entry point in `statement_api.cpp` takes `HandleLock` and clears diagnostics on entry; `SQLFreeStmt` skips the clear on `SQL_DROP`" | ✅ RESOLVED | **WRONG on the first half** | `SQLFreeStmt` (`mock-driver/src/odbc/statement_api.cpp:1644-1677`) takes **no `HandleLock` for any option** — the `fOption != SQL_DROP` guard wraps only `clear_diagnostics()`. `SQL_CLOSE`/`SQL_UNBIND`/`SQL_RESET_PARAMS` mutate containers that a concurrent `SQLFetch` is iterating. 17 of the other 18 entry points do comply. → **D4** |
| §7.10 Promote the `Mode=Success` canary to `failed == 0 && skipped == 0` | ✅ RESOLVED | **SUPERSEDED — not true on Linux** | §8 re-loosened it to `kMaxFailed=6 / kMaxSkipped=23` (`tests/e2e/test_e2e_scenarios.cpp:79-80`). The contract holds on Windows/macOS only, and the "improvement detector" only writes to stderr, so the ratchet can never tighten itself. → **D1**, **B5** |
| §4.1 "Add static analysis (clang-tidy or CodeQL)" | ✅ RESOLVED | **PARTIALLY** | CodeQL runs and is correctly scoped. But the project's own `/W4 /WX` + `-Wall -Wextra -Wconversion` interface reaches only **3 of 8** targets: `src/reporting/`, `src/main.cpp`, `tests/` and **all of `mock-driver/`** compile with default warnings. Rebuilding with `/W4` surfaces 9 real warnings. → **E1** |
| §1.4 `verify_rows_persisted` closes the silent-corruption blind spot | ✅ RESOLVED | **PARTIALLY** | The helper exists and the canaries prove it trips. But it reads into `char buf[256]` and accepts `SQL_SUCCESS_WITH_INFO`, so a value ≥256 chars is silently truncated into a CRITICAL false mismatch; NULL rows are stored as `std::string()`, indistinguishable from `""`, though the header advertises it for NULL-vs-empty work (`test_base.cpp:82-93`). Used by one file only. → **A8** |
| "Weakness #1: silent-corruption blind spot → closed" | summary claim | **OVERSTATED** | The *round-trip* blind spot is closed. The larger one is untouched: a probe that executes fine and reads back a **wrong value** reports `SKIP_INCONCLUSIVE`, which does not affect the exit code. → **A1** |
| §1.1 "Full int→string / float→string round-trip **matrix**" | ✅ RESOLVED | **OVERSTATED** | The two templates exist and are well built, but 9 of ~18 cells are present. CHAR and WVARCHAR are exercised with `SQL_C_SLONG` only (1 of 4 integer C types); floats only against VARCHAR; no unsigned C types. The WVARCHAR cell reads back through `SQL_C_CHAR`, so the W path is half-covered. → **A20** |
| §1.7 `test_sqldescribeparam_{varchar,integer,decimal}` | ✅ RESOLVED | **PARTIALLY** | All three exist — and **none asserts anything**. Each prints "(expected SQL_VARCHAR=… or SQL_WVARCHAR=…)" beside the actual value and never compares them (`param_binding_tests.cpp:850-857, 934-941, 1154-1161`). The claim delivered probe names, not probes. → **B1** |
| §1.6 `test_varchar_raw_byte_integrity` "informational (always PASS)" | ✅ RESOLVED | **CORRECT for that cell; pattern unmanaged** | Its rationale justifies dropping *one* assertion, not all of them — `ind == 8` and `memcmp(raw,"ABCDEFGH",8)` hold on every engine. Meanwhile **at least 26 other probes** always PASS without saying so, and all count toward the advertised pass rate. → **B1**, **B2** |
| PORT §4.6 "driver-detected per-row failure" probe | ✅ RESOLVED | **WRONG (first half)** | `test_param_status_per_row_partial_failure` cannot detect a driver-detected failure: `ODBC_TEST_ARRAY` is created with no PK, no UNIQUE and no NOT NULL (`array_param_tests.cpp:47`), so no row can violate anything and the probe green-paths to PASS on every real driver. Its sibling `test_paramset_size_unsupported_returns_error` **is** genuinely good. → **B1** |
| PORT §4.12 "**consolidated** scalar-function claim-vs-execute matrix" | ✅ RESOLVED | **PARTIALLY** | The matrix probe exists and works, but it was *added alongside* the four per-category probes rather than consolidating them — the bitmask walk and query table are now duplicated five ways. It also has an empty `if` body (`escape_sequence_tests.cpp:1145-1147`) and a `total_passed` that is computed and never read. → **C12** |
| §8.1 Linux diagnostics: "likely cause — unixODBC pulls via legacy `SQLError`, which the mock does not export" | ❌ OPEN (hypothesis) | **HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG, AND ALL THREE REAL CAUSES ARE NOW FIXED** | The rejection stands and is worth keeping: `SQLError` genuinely does not exist in the mock, but it cannot have been the cause, because unixODBC falls back to the 2.x path only when *neither* `SQLGetDiagRec` nor `SQLGetDiagRecW` resolves and the Linux `.so` exports both. `SQLError` was never implemented, and nothing needed it. **The three code-verified causes ranked ahead of it have since been fixed, and re-checked here rather than assumed:** (1) the `row_count_` shadowing is gone - `handles.hpp:167` now carries a comment where the duplicate member used to be, and `diagnostic_api.cpp` reads the one remaining field (**D14**); the four fields that were never written anywhere - `return_code_`, `dynamic_function_`, `dynamic_function_code_`, `cursor_row_count_` - all have writers now (**D22**). (2) Registration parity: `register.sh` writes `APILevel`, `ConnectFunctions`, `DriverODBCVer` and `SQLLevel`, which was the one input that differed between the passing Windows slot and the failing Linux one. (3) Clearing diagnostics on entry is not a defect - the ODBC spec requires it, and it is per-handle, so a DM-internal call on the connection cannot wipe a statement’s stack. **The `ltrace` step the row proposed is moot**: it was there to discriminate between (2) and (3), and (2) is fixed while (3) turned out not to be a fault. **Evidence it is closed:** the e2e suite runs green on Linux and macOS in CI, and all four rows this pointed at - **D2**, **D3**, **D21**, **D22** - are DONE. |
| §7.1 Verify the postgresql + mariadb `apt_version` pins | ❌ OPEN | **VERIFIED — and the verification now recurs instead of waiting to be remembered** | The pins are `1:16.00.0000-1` and `3.1.15-3`, and they have run: stress-test run **34187930345** (2026-09-08) was green on **all seven** drivers, postgresql and mariadb included, so the row’s "the new pins have never run" is no longer true. **The check is structural, not a one-off reading**: `apt-get install "odbc-postgresql=${APT_VERSION}"` fails outright when Ubuntu drops the pinned version, and the step after it re-reads `dpkg -s` and emits `::error::Installed ... but manifest pinned ...` followed by `exit 1` if what landed disagrees. Both named drivers have that guard, and so does mysql. **What was actually missing, and is the fix here:** nothing ever ran it. `stress-test.yml` was `workflow_dispatch` only, so a pin rots silently until someone happens to dispatch - which is exactly how pins came to change and never be exercised. It now also runs on a **monthly schedule** (06:00 on the 1st). **The trap that came with it**: every job is gated on the `driver` input naming either `all` or that one driver, and a scheduled run supplies no inputs at all - so all seven jobs would have skipped and the run would have reported success. A monthly green tick testing nothing is the same do-nothing-and-say-nothing class as **D26** and **D87**, so all seven gates now spell the absent-input default out as `all`. → **F1** |
| §7.3 Decide the fate of `fetch-stress-test.ps1` | ❌ OPEN | **RESOLVED BY H11 — the drift cannot recur, because the copy is gone** | The row’s two symptoms were a `$Drivers` hashtable with no `firebird` entry while the manifest had one, and output written to a `recommendations/` directory that no longer existed. Both are gone, and not by being patched: **H11** deleted the script’s private copy of the clone/download/gate logic and made it call `.claude/skills/triage-driver/triage.ps1` once per driver instead. **The firebird drift is now structurally impossible** - there is no hashtable to fall behind; the driver list is `$Manifest.drivers.PSObject.Properties.Name`, read from `.github/drivers.json`, so a driver added to the manifest is in the script the moment it is added. That is a better answer than a test, which could only have caught the two lists disagreeing after the fact. `recommendations/` survives only in the header comment, as the record of what the script used to do. → **F5** |
| §5.1 Retire the 23 wrappers; add the e2e harness | ✅ RESOLVED | **CONFIRMED** | Wrappers gone, `tests/unit` + `tests/e2e` split real, 14 scenarios pass locally. No action. |
| §2.11 Remove the `TestStatus::SKIP` alias | ✅ RESOLVED | **CONFIRMED** | No remnant; all three `switch`es enumerate every value. Footnote: the `is_skipped()` helper added for it has **zero call sites**. → **C6** |
| §3.3 `read_param_value` pointer guards | ✅ RESOLVED | **CONFIRMED** | Element-size, stride and `strnlen`-with-1 MB-cap guards all present and correct (`statement_api.cpp:76, 82, 178-189, 202-208`). |
| §3.12 "CI checks every `.def` export has an implementation" | ✅ RESOLVED | **CONFIRMED but one-directional — and it hid a bigger problem** | The check verifies `.def → source` only. The reverse is unchecked, and **38 `SQL_API` functions are defined in the sources but absent from the `.def`**. Its extraction regex also silently skips any export not indented with exactly four spaces. → **D2**, **F11** |
| §3.13 "`FailOn` honoured by the 8 catalog functions" | ✅ RESOLVED | **CONFIRMED, but inert by default** | All eight call sites exist (`catalog_api.cpp:215, 274, 364, 437, 499, 567, 642, 681`). However `DriverConfig::should_fail` returns `false` unconditionally for `BehaviorMode::Success` (`config.cpp:30-31`) — the default — so `FailOn` does nothing unless `Mode=Partial` is also set. The main `README.md:205` says so; `mock-driver/README.md:26` does not even list `Partial` as a valid `Mode`. → **D26** |
| §3.7 "Connection-string parser hardened; 11 adversarial tests" | ✅ RESOLVED | **PARTIALLY** | The 11 tests exist and pass, but `in_braces` is a `bool` toggled by a `{` *anywhere*, not only at value start (`config.cpp:67,70-75`): `Key=a{b;Mode=Failure` swallows the rest of the string and `Mode` is never parsed. The ODBC `}}` escape is unhandled. And `Latency=10s` silently means 10 **ms**, `Latency=500us` means 0 ms (`config.cpp:189-191`). None of these is covered by the 11 tests. → **D27** |
| §7.8 UPDATE/DELETE real row counting; §7.9 multi-tuple INSERT | ✅ RESOLVED | **CONFIRMED** | `make_where_predicate` and `split_value_tuples` are both correct as described. Caveats tracked separately (**D11**, **D12**). |
| §3.5 `~StatementHandle` frees only `SQL_DESC_ALLOC_AUTO` descriptors | ✅ RESOLVED | **CONFIRMED but dead code, guarding the wrong direction** | `SQL_DESC_ALLOC_USER` is never assigned anywhere, and `SQLSetStmtAttr` cannot accept `SQL_ATTR_APP_ROW_DESC`, so the guarded scenario is unreachable. The *reachable* double-free — an app freeing an implicit descriptor via `SQLFreeHandle` — is unguarded. → **D8** |
| §3.10 WONTFIX data-driven mock schema; §4.6 WONTFIX install headers | 🚫 WONTFIX | **CONFIRMED reasonable** | No action. |

## Part A — Probe result integrity

The tool's entire value is that its verdicts are trustworthy. These are cases where a verdict is wrong. **Highest priority.**

| ID | Status | Sev | Area | Task | Details / Evidence | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Classification | **Stop classifying a wrong value as SKIP** in dialect-fallback loops | With **C2**'s helper in place, a variant that executes makes the probe conclusive: the comparison happens and a mismatch is a FAIL, instead of falling through to the next dialect and ending as `SKIP_INCONCLUSIVE` — which does not affect the exit code. Applied to the scope this row names: **all 9 DataType probes** and `statement_tests.cpp`'s `test_parameter_binding`. Each now also distinguishes "executed but returned no row" from "returned the wrong value", and reports what it got against what it expected. **Measured end to end**: under `SilentCorruption=SkewNumeric` the suite was 158 pass / **33 fail / 4 skip** before and is 158 / **36 / 1** after — exactly three DataType probes moving from SKIP to FAIL, with messages like `Expected 42, got 43 (indicator=4)` in place of "No compatible query pattern found". **Test**: e2e scenario `WrongValueIsAFailureNotAnInconclusiveSkip` asserts all three are FAIL under that configuration and that `actual` names the value, so a regression to the old behaviour is caught by verdict *and* by message. `test_parameter_binding` needed the general `try_first_working` form — see **C2** — and fixing it surfaced a real mock defect: only a *bare* `?` was recognised as a parameter marker, so `SELECT CAST(? AS INTEGER)` came back as the literal string `"?"`. The mock accepted the query, executed it, and returned the wrong value; the probe was right and the fixture was wrong. Fixed in `parse_literal_expression` (the literal-SELECT corner of **D10**), verified with a standalone ODBC program against all three variants before and after. | bab9733 |
| A2 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Portability | **58 bare `SELECT <expr>` statements have no Firebird fallback** — four probe categories are unusable against the driver family this repo serves | Fixed once in the shared helper, as the task directed, rather than in 58 string literals: `TestBase::literal_select_variants(sql)` returns the bare form, `sql + " FROM RDB$DATABASE"` (Firebird) and `sql + " FROM DUAL"` (Oracle, and MySQL accepts it), and `execute_first_working` picks whichever runs. All four files routed through it: **escape_sequence_tests** — all 46 sites go through `exec_scalar()`, fixed in that one function; **numeric_struct_tests** — 6 direct `stmt.execute()` calls, via a new `execute_literal_select()` that throws when no variant works, so the probes keep relying on `run_test`'s catch exactly as before; **transaction_tests** — the single site at `:588`, inside the CRITICAL rollback-with-open-cursor probe; **cursor_stress_tests** — 5 sites, of which the two 500-iteration timing loops resolve the working variant *once* before the loop, because probing three dialects inside the loop would have been measured as part of the open phase that **A12** compares. **Test**: 4 unit cases pinning the variant list — bare form first (so the common case stays one round trip), Firebird and Oracle both present, the expression preserved verbatim (the escape probes pass whole `{fn …}` expressions through), and no duplicates. **No end-to-end test is possible from this host**: the mock accepts a bare `SELECT`, so the Firebird branch is never taken here and there is no Firebird toolchain on this machine. The mechanism is covered by the unit tests plus **C2**'s proven `execute_first_working`; the real confirmation will be the firebird stress-test job, where these three categories should stop reporting ERROR / "cursor exhaustion issues" / `'NULL'`. Also marked the Appendix's A2 counting command obsolete: the bare literals are still in the source, but as arguments to the helper rather than as statements sent to a driver. | 9446e8f |
| A3 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Buffer safety | Driver-supplied lengths used as buffer lengths — stack over-read on truncation | Added `TestBase::bounded_string(buf, capacity, reported)` returning `{value, truncated, length_unknown}`, and routed all four listed sites through it: `escape_sequence_tests.cpp:66` (`SQLNativeSql`'s `StringLength2Ptr`) and `:81` (`SQLGetData`'s indicator), `connection_tests.cpp:32` and `:56` (`SQLGetInfo`). The helper clamps to `capacity-1`, treats `SQL_NO_TOTAL` as "unknown, assume more" rather than letting -4 become `SIZE_MAX-3`, treats any other negative as no-value-to-trust, and never reads past the first NUL the driver actually wrote — using `memchr`, so an unterminated buffer is bounded rather than undefined. **Searched repo-wide** for the same shape afterwards: `std::string(buf, <driver-length>)` no longer appears anywhere in `src/tests/`. Four single-argument `std::string(cat_buf)` constructions remain in `metadata_tests.cpp:107,108,366,367`; those depend on NUL-termination rather than on a reported length, so they are a different failure mode and belong to **C7**, which consolidates the four hand-rolled "SQLGetData into a std::string" variants. Truncation is surfaced where the call site can act on it (both `connection_tests` probes append "(truncated; driver reported N bytes)" to `actual`); `exec_scalar` returns `nullopt` on truncation for now, because a truncated value cannot be compared against an expected one and the helper's `optional<string>` signature has no channel to say why — **A4** replaces that signature with `{value, sqlstate, diagnostic}`, at which point truncation becomes a reportable outcome rather than silence. **Test**: 8 exhaustive unit cases in `tests/unit/test_test_base.cpp` (`BoundedStringTest`) — exact length, empty, reported-past-buffer, `SQL_NO_TOTAL`, `SQL_NULL_DATA`, unterminated buffer, over-reported-within-buffer, null buffer / zero capacity. Made the helper `public` on `TestBase` so it can be tested as the pure function it is. Also verified end to end under `BufferValidation=Lenient` (**D33**), where `SQLGetInfo` returns an unterminated string: both connection probes still report the correct value instead of trailing garbage. | a5e8250 |
| A4 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Diagnostics | The escape-sequence helpers throw away every SQLSTATE | `exec_scalar` returned `std::optional<std::string>` behind `catch (...) { return std::nullopt; }`, so a failed scalar function reached the report as `UCASE='NULL'` — no SQLSTATE, no failing SQL. That is why the DuckDB triage had to be resolved by reading driver source to find `SetNotImplemented`/`HYC00`. Added `exec_scalar_ex()` returning `ScalarResult {value, sqlstate, message, query, truncated}`; `exec_scalar()` remains as a thin wrapper for the call sites that only want the value. The result carries the query that *actually ran*, which after **A2** may be a dialect variant rather than the one passed in. Two file-local helpers use it: `describe_scalar_failure()` renders a failure as "UCASE failed with 42000 (…the failing SQL…)" instead of `UCASE='NULL'`, and `scalar_unsupported()` applies the same four states **B3** uses. **Fed into the verdict, as the row asks**: a function the driver's bitmask *claims* and then reports `HYC00` for is counted as `unsupported` rather than as a wrong answer, and a probe where every claimed function is unimplemented is `SKIP_UNSUPPORTED` rather than FAIL — while anything else stays a FAIL. **Corrected my own first attempt**: it decremented `tested`, which drove the count to zero and made the probe report "Driver claims no string function support" — precisely the wrong message, since the driver *had* claimed them. A separate counter keeps the two findings distinct. **Verified in both directions** against the mock: with `ErrorCode=HYC00` the probes report `SKIP_UNSUPPORTED` — "Driver claims string functions but implements none: UCASE claimed but not implemented (HYC00); …"; with `ErrorCode=42000` they report `FAIL` — "UCASE failed with 42000 (  SELECT {fn UCASE('hello')}  ->  42000: …)", naming both the state and the SQL. Also replaced the two remaining `oss << t.name << "=NULL; "` sites, which were the literal symptom this row describes. | dc01b6f |
| A5 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Probe logic | `test_null_termination` cannot detect what it tests, and over-reads the stack when it should fire | `buffer_validation_tests.cpp` now finds the terminator with `std::memchr(buffer, 0, sizeof buffer)` instead of `std::strlen`. The old code was undefined behaviour in exactly the case the probe exists to detect — the buffer is `memset` to `'X'` with no NUL, so `strlen` read off the end of the stack whenever the driver failed to terminate — and its FAIL branch, `buffer[strlen(buffer)] != '\0'`, was a tautology and therefore unreachable. A null `memchr` result is now a FAIL at `Severity::ERR` naming the buffer size and the driver-reported length; otherwise the NUL offset is compared against `buffer_length` as before. **Test**: e2e scenario `NullTerminationProbeCatchesAnUnterminatedString` — PASS at `Mode=Success`, FAIL with "No NUL within 256 bytes" under `BufferValidation=Lenient`, which **D33** had to make functional first. **Honest note on what the fix bought**: under Lenient the *old* code would probably also have reported FAIL, but by accident — via out-of-bounds reads producing a garbage length that happened not to match, at `WARNING` severity with a misleading message. The fix removes the UB and makes the correct branch reachable by design rather than by luck. | 6ccab56 |

The floor is now on the *close* phase and yields a **PASS**: the question is "is SQLCloseCursor pathologically slow?", and a close phase under 1 ms in total across 500 cycles answers no at any ratio, so a measured "not pathological" is a real verdict rather than an absence of one. New `TestBase::get_data_full()` calls `SQLGetData` repeatedly until the value is whole, using **A3**'s `bounded_string` to decide what each call actually wrote and continuing only on the truncation shape (`SQL_SUCCESS_WITH_INFO` plus a reported length that overshoots the buffer, or `SQL_NO_TOTAL`) rather than on the diagnostics, so an unrelated warning cannot spin it. **NULL**: `actual_values` is now `vector<optional<string>>` — it was `vector<string>`, in which NULL and `''` were the same value, in the one helper the header advertises for NULL-versus-empty work. `RowVerification::display(i)` renders NULL as `<NULL>` for the probes that print into `actual`; the two comparing sites in `param_binding_tests.cpp` report "got NULL" instead of "got ''". **Identifier quoting — the task's stated fix is wrong and was not applied.** Quoting all three names with `SQL_IDENTIFIER_QUOTE_CHAR` broke 13 probes here, and working through why showed it would break real engines too: every table this tool creates is created *unquoted*, and an unquoted name is folded by the engine — **up** by Firebird and Oracle, **down** by PostgreSQL. Quoting only the SELECT side asks PostgreSQL for `"ODBC_TEST_PARAM"` when it stored `odbc_test_param`, introducing a failure where there was none; quoting is only safe when both ends agree, and the DDL side is spread across `RoundTripTableGuard` and three separate `create_test_table` implementations. The hazard the row actually names cannot arise for a bare-safe name, and a name that is *not* bare-safe cannot have been created unquoted in the first place — so the rule implemented is: leave bare-safe names alone, quote the rest. New `is_bare_identifier()` decides, deliberately stricter than any engine's rule since erring towards "needs quoting" is the safe way to be wrong. **Two findings came out of this**, both filed rather than absorbed: the mock did not implement the `SQLGetData` continuation protocol at all (**D37**, fixed here because A19 is untestable without it), and `register.ps1` wrote a relative `-DriverPath` to the registry verbatim (**D39**, fixed here because it is why the new tests skipped). A third, the mock advertising a quote character it cannot parse, is **D38**. **Test**: `tests/unit/test_row_verification.cpp`, 7 cases — two pure ones pinning `is_bare_identifier`, and five against the mock covering a 700-character round trip through both `get_data_full` and `verify_rows_persisted`, a short value read exactly once, NULL versus `''` in the same result set, and bare-safe names surviving unquoted. **Proven, not assumed**: the mock was temporarily reverted to restart the value on every `SQLGetData` (its pre-D37 behaviour), and both long-value tests failed with `driver did not advance between SQLGetData calls (still reports 700 bytes available after 510 were read)` in 4 ms — which simultaneously demonstrates that the loop's non-advancing-driver guard terminates instead of wedging the run. | A20 | ✅ DONE | MED | Coverage | Finish the round-trip matrix and verify the array string axis | **Matrix**: 7 cells added, 9 → 16. Plus `SQL_C_SBIGINT` and `SQL_C_DOUBLE` against `SQL_CHAR`, and `SQL_C_SBIGINT` against `SQL_WVARCHAR`, since both of those axes had only `SQL_C_SLONG` — a driver could get the 32-bit case right and the 64-bit or floating case wrong on those column types with nothing looking. **Proved falsifiable**: under `SilentCorruption=SkewNumeric` all 7 new cells fail alongside the 9 existing ones, 17 round-trip failures in total. **Not done**: reading the WVARCHAR cell back through `SQL_C_WCHAR`. `verify_rows_persisted` reads with `SQL_C_CHAR`, so covering the W fetch path means either a W variant of that helper or a separate probe, and it belongs with the W-path work rather than bolted onto the matrix. The row's other four items are done, so this is the one part left and it is stated rather than implied. **Array string axis**: `test_column_wise_array_binding` bound `ID = {100,200,300}` and `NAME = {"Alice","Bob","Charlie"}`, executed, and inspected only `exec_ret` — so a driver that ran one parameter set instead of three, or wrote "Alice" three times, passed. It now reads the rows back with `verify_rows_persisted` (the first call to it in this file, which the row notes was zero) and compares both columns. **Proved twice**: `DropInserts` makes it report `COUNT(*) = 0 but expected 3`, and temporarily expecting "Alice" three times makes it report `row 2: expected 'Alice' got 'Bob'` — so the count check and the string comparison each fire independently. | A21 | ✅ DONE | MED | Probe logic | Several probes accept the wrong answer | All five sites fixed. **(1)** `test_string_as_integer` — both comparison branches set PASS, so `0` for `'123'` passed; now compares against 123, treats `SQL_NULL_DATA` as its own failure, and explains that SQL_CHAR→SQL_C_SLONG is a required Core conversion. **(2)** `test_varchar_empty` accepted `SQL_NULL_DATA` as "empty string", because `strlen(buffer)==0` is true for the untouched zero-initialised buffer a NULL fetch leaves behind — the exact conflation its sibling `test_null_vs_empty_distinction_varchar` FAILs; NULL is now its own FAIL and the PASS path requires `indicator == 0` **and** an actually-empty buffer. **(3)** `test_varchar_special_chars` retrieved the value and never compared it; it now compares, via `bounded_string` (**A3**). Now parsed properly for the integer and floating C types, with `22018` for a value that genuinely cannot be cast; the remaining C-type gaps stay **D11**'s and unifying the four independent C-type switches stays **D18**'s. (b) `SQLGetInfo`/`SQLGetInfoW` returned `SQL_SUCCESS_WITH_INFO` on truncation while posting no diagnostic at all, so an application could not tell truncation from any other warning — `01004` is now posted on both paths; the other five callers `string_utils` leaves silent stay **D24**'s. **Test coverage, stated precisely.** Four new mock unit tests cover the mock fixes (`SELECT '123'`→123, `'2.5'`→2.5, `'abc'`→`SQL_ERROR`+22018, truncated `SQLGetInfo` posts 01004) — and because probes (1) and (5) now assert exactly those behaviours, a regression on either side breaks 195/195. | A27 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Probe logic | **A discarded `SQLGetData` return code turned a broken driver into a PASS** — `test_manual_rollback` reported "Transaction rolled back successfully" for a driver whose `SQLGetData` returned `SQL_ERROR` | Found while doing **A22** at the same two statements (`transaction_tests.cpp`, the `SELECT COUNT(*)` checks in `test_manual_commit` and `test_manual_rollback`), but a distinct defect from A22's "no `else` on the fetch", so it is filed separately rather than widening that row. ---

## Part B — Result taxonomy and reporting honesty

`README.md` advertises a pass rate. Today it is inflated by probes that cannot fail and deflated by probes that SKIP when they should FAIL.

| ID | Status | Sev | Area | Task | Details / Evidence | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Taxonomy | **At least 26 probes PASS on every branch** — audit and fix | **The audit found 55 always-PASS probes, not "at least 26"** — counted mechanically as a `run_test` body containing no `TestStatus::FAIL`, no `report_failure` and no way to reach either. All but two are now able to fail or are declared `INFORMATIONAL`; the two left are accounted for below. The recurring shapes, in the order they cost the most: **(1) A Core function that skipped on failure.** `SQLPrepare`, `SQLNumResultCols`, `SQLDescribeCol`, `SQLCloseCursor`, `SQLBindCol`, `SQLRowCount`, `SQLNumParams`, `SQLTables`, `SQLGetInfo(SQL_DATABASE_NAME)`, `SQLGetConnectAttr(SQL_ATTR_AUTOCOMMIT)`, `SQLGetConnectAttr(SQL_ATTR_TXN_ISOLATION)`, `SQLGetDiagField(SQL_DIAG_ROW_COUNT)`, the four implicit descriptors — twenty-odd probes reported "not supported" or "inconclusive" for a function every driver must implement. Several said so while their *own suggestion text* named the function as Core; `test_connection_info`'s suggestion read "SQLGetInfo succeeded but database name unavailable" on the branch where it had not succeeded. **(2) A second PASS in the `else`.** `SQLBindParameter` with a null pointer and `SQL_NULL_DATA` (the spec's documented way to pass NULL); `SQLNumParams` returning any count at all; `SQLMoreResults` returning `SQL_SUCCESS` after a single-result-set query, which claims a result set that is not there; a descriptor field that accepts a write and reads back differently; `SQLNumResultCols` answering while `SQLDescribeCol` fails, meaning the IRD is populated half way; `SQLGetDiagRec` returning `SQL_ERROR` and being reported as "SQLGetDiagRec functional". **(3) A skip justified by the call having failed rather than by a SQLSTATE** — the five Level 1/2 catalog probes, `SQLNativeSql`, `SQLDescribeParam`, `SQLColumnsW`, `SQLProcedures`, the privilege pair, `SQLBindParameter(SQL_C_WCHAR)`, and the scrollable-cursor probes, all routed through **B3**'s `report_failure` now: `HYC00`/`IM001`/`HY106` still skip, everything else is the failure it is. This is **B4**'s rule, applied here because making a probe able to fail *is* B1's job and doing it once beats doing half of it twice. **(4) Accepted and ignored.** `SQLSetStmtAttr` returning success and then reporting the old value was a `SKIP_UNSUPPORTED`; the spec allows rejecting the attribute or substituting with `01S02`, not silently keeping the old value, and an application has no way to discover the difference. **(5) Printed `expected` beside `actual` and never compared** — the three `SQLDescribeParam` probes. Comparing immediately failed the reference driver, which turned out to be **D45**: the mock answered `VARCHAR(255)` for every parameter of every statement. That is *why* the comparison was missing. **Nine probes are `INFORMATIONAL`** because there is genuinely nothing to grade: an empty SQL string (implementation-defined), an optional connection timeout and pooling mode (every value legal), the type `COUNT(*)` comes back as (engine's choice — the row **B2** names by name), whether a driver needs `SQLFreeStmt(SQL_CLOSE)` between executes (both behaviours conformant, and which one matters to a bulk consumer), the CONVERT capability census, and the three escape probes named for escapes they never send. **Two left, both deliberate**: `test_columns_catalog`'s skip is honest — `SQLColumns` *succeeded* and the catalog had nothing to show, which is a fact about the account's permissions and not about the driver, and the Core-ness of the call is covered by it succeeding; and the detector's last hit is an artifact of its paren matching on the rewritten `Valid Transitions Test`, which has three failure branches. **New rows**: **D42**, **D43**, **D44** (the escape redesign and the two fixture gaps blocking it) and **D45**. **Pass rate**: 186 PASS / 9 INFORMATIONAL / 186 scored / **100%**, from 195/195. Redone **H1** in the same commit as the phase requires. | b23d449 |
| B2 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Taxonomy | Add a status that is reported but excluded from the pass rate | New `TestStatus::INFORMATIONAL`, reported like any other result and excluded from the pass rate's **denominator** rather than counted as a pass. Named `INFORMATIONAL` and not `INFO` because `Severity::INFO` already exists and the two would read as the same thing at a call site. Touched, as the row said it would have to be, in one go: the enum and `status_to_string` in `test_base.hpp`; `main.cpp`'s `tally_results`, `run_test_category` and the summary call; `Reporter`'s virtual and both implementations. **The denominator is published, not implied**: the JSON summary gains `informational` and `scored`, and `pass_rate` is now `passed / scored`. `total_tests` keeps its old meaning, so a consumer reading `pass_rate` is unaffected while one that recomputed `passed / total_tests` will disagree with us — which is exactly why `scored` is in the document rather than left to be inferred. The console prints `100.0% of 195 scored` rather than a bare percentage, and adds an `Informational: N (reported, not scored)` line only when there are any. Per-result tag is `[INFO]`, deliberately distinct from `[PASS]`: the point of the status is that nothing was graded, and a reader scanning the column must see that without reading the text. **No probe uses it yet** — that is **B1** and **A23**, next in this phase — so the mock is still 195 PASS / 0 informational / 195 scored, and the advertised rate has not moved. **Test**: four unit cases and one e2e assertion, split by what each can actually prove. `InformationalResultsAreReportedButNotScored` builds a 4-result run (1 PASS, 1 FAIL, 2 informational) and pins 50% — the same run is 25% over `total_tests` and 75% if the informational two are counted as passes, so the assertion distinguishes all three readings rather than just the old one. `AnEntirelyInformationalRunHasNoPassRate` pins that zero scored results is 0%, not 100% and not a division by zero. `InformationalResultsAreNamedAndKeptOutOfThePassRate` covers the console text and that such a run is still "ALL TESTS PASSED". `InformationalHasItsOwnTag` pins `[INFO]` and, importantly, that `[PASS]` is *absent* — it caught the real bug that `status_icon` had not been updated and was printing `[????]`. The e2e assertion covers the wiring the unit tests cannot: that `main.cpp`'s tally reaches the report, and that the published `scored`, `informational`, `passed` and `pass_rate` are self-consistent in a real run. **README updated** in the same commit with the new tag and the denominator rule. | 50c2304 |
| B3 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Classification | Add `TestBase::classify_failure()` and use it everywhere | Added `TestBase::classify_failure(handle_type, handle)` returning `{status, sqlstate, message}` and `report_failure(r, …, what)` which applies it and **always** writes the state into `r.diagnostic`, whichever way it classified. `IM001 / HYC00 / HY092 / HY106` → `SKIP_UNSUPPORTED`; everything else → `FAIL`, with the severity raised to `ERR` if the probe's metadata had left it at `INFO`. It walks the **whole** diagnostic queue rather than reading record 1, because a driver manager may prepend its own record — unixODBC's `IM006` is the one seen in practice — in front of the driver's; that detail is extracted from `array_param_tests.cpp:1192` and `metadata_tests.cpp:815`, the two sites the task identifies as already correct. **Applied at 21 sites**: `catalog_depth_tests.cpp` ×3 (SQLTables ×2 and SQLStatistics, all Core, all previously `SKIP_INCONCLUSIVE` without reading anything); `diagnostic_depth_tests.cpp` ×1, where a missing record 1 after a `SQL_ERROR` is now a hard FAIL because the spec requires the record to exist; `numeric_struct_tests.cpp` ×10 (`SQL_C_NUMERIC` is a required Core target type, and every failure was being excused); `datatype_edge_tests.cpp` ×3 (including `test_string_as_integer`, where the comment literally read "Some drivers don't support this conversion" about a required Core conversion); `array_param_tests.cpp` ×6 (genuinely optional attributes, so the classification usually still lands on SKIP — but now it is earned); `param_binding_tests.cpp` ×6 (3 × `SQLRowCount`, which is Core, and 3 × `SQLDescribeParam`, which is not). The three `SQLDescribeParam` sites needed the classification captured **inside** the try block, because the statement is destroyed before the check runs and the handle would already be gone. **Verified in both directions**: under `SupportsArrayBind=false` the array attributes still report `SKIP_UNSUPPORTED` and now name `HYC00` in the report; a Core function failing with `42000` is a `FAIL`. **Tests**: 2 unit cases (a handle with no diagnostics classifies as FAIL, because silence is not evidence a feature is unimplemented; and `report_failure` always records a state and lifts INFO to ERR) plus 2 e2e scenarios covering each direction. **Not applied**: ~19 further sites the row's "~40" implies, in files whose SKIPs are about discovered-table availability rather than a failed call — those are **C7**'s and **B4**'s. Every site named explicitly in the row is done. | 2141dd7 |
| B4 | ✅ DONE | MED | Classification | Stop converting infrastructure errors into "driver doesn't support it" | **Most of this row was closed by B1**, and deliberately: making a probe able to fail *is* stopping it from excusing a failure as unsupported, so the two were the same edit at every catalog site and doing it once beat doing half of it twice. It now catches `core::OdbcError` only (which genuinely *is* the driver failing a cycle, which is what the probe measures) and keeps the first one, so the verdict says which failure rather than only how many. Anything else now reaches **A9**'s barrier in `run_test` and is reported as a defect in this tool, which is what it is. **`SQLColAttribute(SQL_DESC_UNSIGNED)`** in `metadata_tests.cpp` reported "driver does not implement" for any failure; the field is genuinely optional, so the skip stays — but only for HYC00, HY091 or IM001. **The two in-file NOTEs** the row cites at `:435-436` and `:557` described the downgrade as preserved behaviour; they now describe the fix, since a comment that documents a bug the code no longer has is worse than no comment. | a857f6d |
| B5 | ✅ DONE | MED | Test architecture | Make the Linux e2e tolerance a ratchet that can tighten | **Implemented as the row specified**: dropping *below* baseline now fails. It reads oddly to fail a build for an improvement, and that is the point: the failure is fixed by editing one number, and nothing else forces that number down. **Proved locally**, which needed a trick worth recording: the block is `#if defined(__linux__) \|\| defined(__APPLE__)` and this host is Windows, where the observed count is 0. Reverted immediately; the guard is back and both configurations build clean. **Order 48 asked for the constants to be deleted once the count reached zero. It cannot reach zero.** Three probes fail on both POSIX platforms because their driver managers write a terminator past the declared buffer length - **D1**, **D59** - and no change to this repository can alter that. | B6 | ✅ DONE | MED | Classification | Report partial success instead of discarding it | The loop over three (precision, scale) variants returned on the first problem, discarding every variant that had already byte-matched. The distinction that makes this worth doing: a precision the engine cannot declare has removed a variant from the probe, while a variant that came back wrong is a finding — the old code could not tell those apart because it returned on both. `actual` now leads with `N/3 variants byte-match` and lists the not-declarable and wrong sets separately. **Proved**: under `SilentCorruption=SkewNumeric` it reports `0/3` and goes on to describe each wrong variant, where the old code stopped at the first; at `Mode=Success` it reports `3/3` with each variant's accepted scale. Only the *correct*-answer branch is driver-manager guaranteed; a deviation has to stay gradeable, and it does. **The conformance tag**: `catalog_depth_tests.cpp` tagged `SQLStatistics` as `LEVEL_1`. It is in the ODBC 3.x **Core** interface-conformance list, so every report this tool has written has described a Core gap as an optional one. Now `CORE`. **Test**: the tag and the verdict are both covered by the 202-result reference run, in which `test_execute_without_prepare` and `Invalid Operation Test` must now agree — a future edit that makes one of them lenient again shows up as a mismatch between two probes of the same call rather than as a silent inconsistency. | a857f6d |
| B8 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Reporting | Fix misleading `expected` text, names and spec references | Every item the row lists, and one it did not. **`test_cursor_types`** was named for enumerating cursor types, tagged with `SQLSetStmtAttr`, and described as "Query supported cursor types" — it reads `SQL_ATTR_CURSOR_TYPE` off a fresh statement and neither sets a type nor enumerates anything. | B9 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Naming | Normalise probe names to `test_snake_case` | 17 probe names normalised across the three files the row names — `buffer_validation_tests` (5), `state_machine_tests` (6), `error_queue_tests` (6). The rename was done by exact-string replacement of the *name* argument only — the `expected` strings in those files are also Title Case sentences and must not be touched, which a blanket regex would have got wrong. **The e2e harness reference was updated with them**: `NullTerminationProbe` looks `"Null Termination Test"` up by exact name in two places, so this would have been a silent skip rather than a compile error. **Verified structurally**: every one of the 202 probe names in the reference report now matches `^test_[a-z0-9_]+$`, checked against the JSON rather than by reading the files. | a3e8416 |
| B10 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Classification | `test_error_clearing` recognises only three hard-coded "old" SQLSTATEs | The probe forced an error, ran a successful statement on the same handle, and then asked whether the leftover SQLSTATE was one of `42000`, `42S02` or `HY000`. Any other leftover — `37000`, `42601`, `HY090`, whatever the engine chose for a bad statement — fell into the `else` and passed as "likely info from new op", which is the opposite of the finding. It now captures the SQLSTATE the forced error actually produced and compares against **that**, so the check works whatever state the driver picks; the passing branch names both states rather than guessing at the second one's provenance. **Test**: covered by the reference run, where the mock's forced error and the comparison are the same value — a regression that reintroduced a hard-coded list would still pass there, which is worth saying, but the shape that made this a bug (an engine whose state is not in the list) is now structurally impossible rather than dependent on the list being complete. | a3e8416 |
| B11 | ✅ DONE | MED | Coverage | Three `SQLGetDiagField` header fields no probe ever reads | **Done.** Three probes in `diagnostic_depth_tests.cpp`: `test_diagfield_return_code`, `test_diagfield_dynamic_function` and `test_diagfield_cursor_row_count`. **Proved against the pre-D22 driver** (a checkout of `mock-driver/src` at `a471f88`, which reported fiction in all three fields): `test_diagfield_dynamic_function` FAILs with `SQL_DIAG_DYNAMIC_FUNCTION_CODE = 0 (expected 85, SQL_DIAG_SELECT_CURSOR)` — 0 being `SQL_DIAG_UNKNOWN_STATEMENT`, which the suggestion points out is rarely true of the statement the driver just parsed and executed. **`test_diagfield_return_code` is INFORMATIONAL, and that was measured rather than assumed.** Against the pre-D22 mock, which deliberately reported `SQL_SUCCESS` in that field, the probe still read `SQL_ERROR` — so on Windows the driver manager answers `SQL_DIAG_RETURNCODE` itself and an agreement is a fact about the stack, not about the driver. Rather than leave that unproven, the mock's `cursor_row_count_` was temporarily skewed by +7: the probe FAILs with `SQL_DIAG_CURSOR_ROW_COUNT = 8; rows actually fetched = 1`. **The e2e harness caught a bug in one of these probes**, which is worth recording: `test_diagfield_cursor_row_count` counted rows with `while (SQLFetch(...) == SQL_SUCCESS)`, so under `Warnings=EveryRow` it stopped at the first row and then blamed the driver for a count the cursor did have. `PerRowWarningsDoNotTruncateFetchLoops` failed on it — `failed: 1 vs 0` — exactly the mistake that scenario exists to catch. Fixed to `SQL_SUCCEEDED`. **Reference-run total moves 203 → 206**: 188 PASS + 18 INFORMATIONAL, still 100% of scored. Three new probes and one new informational; the Appendix is updated to match. | a8390e6 |

---

## Part C — Probe infrastructure and duplication

`src/tests/` is 15.4k lines across 23 categories; 8 files exceed 500 lines and `param_binding_tests.cpp` is 2,033. Most of the bulk is one of six copy-pasted shapes. Realistic total saving: **~1,800 LoC (≈12%)**, about a third of it pure boilerplate with no behavioural risk.

| C1 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Boilerplate | Finish the §2.1 migration — `param_binding_tests.cpp` is essentially unmigrated | All 14 hand-rolled bodies migrated: `run_test` goes 3 → 17, `make_result` and the `start_time`/`elapsed` lambda pairs go to **0**, and the 61 `result.duration = elapsed(); return result;` exits become plain `return;`. **−187 LoC** against the row's estimate of −145. Done by script rather than by hand, because the shape was identical in all 14 — the script asserts each `make_result` call is the 8-argument form with `TestStatus::PASS` and an empty `actual` (i.e. exactly what `run_test` constructs internally) *before* rewriting anything, so a body that differed would have stopped it rather than been silently converted. Every probe gains what only three had: `run_test`'s **A9** barrier, so a `std::bad_alloc` or a `std::out_of_range` in any of these 24 probes now costs one ERR result instead of unwinding through `crash_guard` to `main`'s `return 3` and discarding all 195. **Proof that the refactor changed nothing else**: the JSON report was captured before and after and compared probe by probe — same 195 probes, **no status changes, and not one byte of `actual` text differs**. That is a stronger check than 195/195, which would have passed even if two probes had swapped verdicts. **Three redundant `catch (const core::OdbcError&)` blocks left in place, deliberately** — at `:1282`, `:1341` and `:1395` the body is now exactly what `run_test` does, but each is followed by a `drop_roundtrip_table()` outside the `try`, so deleting the catch would skip the drop on the throwing path. That is **C3**'s pattern and C3's next in the phase; the three sites are recorded there. The other 11 catches stay on their own merits: they add context (`"INSERT phase threw: "`) or clean up before returning. Closes **I9**. | 93d31a9 |

| C4 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Duplication | Adopt `RoundTripTableGuard` in the two files that reimplement it | All three reimplementations gone: `TransactionTests::create_test_table` (87 lines), `ArrayParamTests::create_test_table` (87), `ParameterBindingTests::create_roundtrip_table` (40) and the three matching drop helpers (12 + 14 + 9) — **249 lines of function bodies replaced by 6 adapters**, against the row's estimate of ~205. `RoundTripTableGuard` grew two things it needed before it could stand in for them. **(1) Reuse.** All three probed for the table with `SELECT 1 FROM <t> WHERE 1=0` before creating it, because this tool is pointed at other people's databases where the connected user often cannot `CREATE TABLE`; the guard did not, so adopting it as-was would have lost that. It now reuses and — **A15** — empties on reuse, and `was_reused()` says which happened. `val_ddl()` reports `"pre-existing … column"` for a reused table rather than claiming the DDL that was asked for, because probes print it into their report. **(2) The value column's name.** `ODBC_TEST_ARRAY`'s is `NAME`, not `VAL`; making it a constructor parameter is what let the third copy go. **Divergence from the task text, and why**: the guard is RAII, but these probes create in one function and drop in another, so it is held in a `std::optional` member (`transaction_tests`, `array_param_tests`) or a `std::map` keyed by table name (`param_binding_tests`, which builds a table per type in the round-trip matrix and may hold one open while creating another — `std::map` and not `unordered_map` because the guard is move-*constructible* but not move-assignable, holding a connection reference, and only node-based storage never reassigns an element). The **46 explicit `drop_roundtrip_table` calls stay**: restructuring every probe around a scoped guard is a much larger change than "adopt the guard", and it is not what this row asks for. They are now one-line `erase` calls, and the category object's destructor drops anything a probe forgot — which is new, and strictly better than the previous leak-to-next-run behaviour. **One more A14 leak closed on the way**: `TransactionTests::drop_test_table` still forced autocommit ON and never restored it — the last copy of the defect A14 named, missed by both earlier sweeps because it does not use an `old_ac` variable. The guard's destructor uses `ScopedAutocommitOn`. **Test**: 3 new cases in `tests/unit/test_stale_table_reuse.cpp` pinning the guard's own behaviour — reuse without DDL privileges and empty on arrival, create when absent, and honour the value column's name (proved by inserting into `NAME` rather than asserted from the accessor). The existing 3 A15 cases go through the adapters, so they now exercise the guard end to end. **Not done here**: `escape_sequence_tests.cpp` is named in the row but has no table lifecycle to adopt — it runs scalar `SELECT`s and creates nothing; the row's `grep -c` counted it as a non-adopter, which it is, but there is nothing there to convert. | 553f2c7 |

A table needs an optional seed list plus conditional commit verification, which is most of the 163 lines back. Against a driver that cannot prepare that INSERT at all, the probe **blames row-wise binding for a failed prepare** - a wrong diagnosis in a report a driver developer acts on, which is **D68**'s family, not **D29**'s. **The helper took its shape from the best of the ten, not the most common.** One site already tried `SQLPrepareW` and fell back to `SQLPrepare` before giving up; the other nine gave up on the W failure alone, which for an ANSI-only driver is a skip that need not have happened - and W/ANSI asymmetry is the whole subject of **D2**. Consolidating on the more common shape would have spread the weaker behaviour to all ten. **Arithmetic, since this row is already a record of estimates not surviving contact**: −9 lines net, and that is 74 deleted against 65 added of which ~35 are the helper and its comment. The saving is real and small; the reason to do it was the two discarded return codes. **Test**: `AFailedPrepareNamesTheStatementItCouldNotPrepare` runs `FailOn=SQLPrepare` and asserts every array probe that skips names the INSERT it could not prepare - the eight inline prologues said "Could not prepare parameterized INSERT" or "Could not prepare statement", so which of the two INSERTs failed never reached the report - and that no probe blames row-wise binding while the prepare is what failed. **Verified to fail** by restoring the old generic message. **What is not tested, and why** (per this plan's rule that an untestable fix says so): neither behavioural change is *isolable*.
