But git classifies a file holding a NUL as binary, so `git diff`, `git blame`, `git grep` and ripgrep had all been skipping it **in silence for eleven commits** - the same do-nothing-and-say-nothing class as **D26** and the unparsed `IsolationLevel=` in **I6**, this time aimed at the tooling rather than the driver. **Fixed** by writing the two characters that were meant. **Test:** `tools/check_source_text.py`, run by the lint job - no NUL byte and valid UTF-8 in every tracked text source. **Proved:** it names `test_dml.cpp:259:50` on the unrepaired file and passes on 218 files after. The repaired file now diffs as `1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)` instead of `Bin`. | ee848e7 |
| D85 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Mock | A `SQLGetData` continuation outlived the result set it belonged to | **Found by D83’s first read-back test, and the comment on the state is the finding.** `getdata_offset_` is keyed on (column, row) - **D37** - and `handles.hpp` claimed that key covered everything: *"every path that moves the cursor - SQLFetch, SQLFetchScroll, re-execute, SQLFreeStmt(SQL_CLOSE), the catalog functions - restarts the value without having to remember to reset anything."* **The premise is false.** The key restarts a value when the *coordinates* change; a **new result set** puts column 1 of row 0 exactly where the old one had column 1 of row 0, so the key is unchanged and a finished offset is carried into a different value. Reading the same cell twice - `SELECT V FROM T WHERE ID = ?` in a loop, the commonest shape there is - returned the value, then `SQL_NO_DATA` with the caller’s buffer untouched. **The worse case is not the empty one.** An offset landing *inside* a longer value is not exhausted, so the call succeeds and returns the value with its leading characters gone. `SQLCloseCursor` was the site that forgot, but the fix is not three lines in one function: a result set is now an explicit boundary, and every site that installs, clears or abandons `result_data_` calls the new `StatementHandle::reset_getdata_continuation()` - the execute paths, the array-parameter path, `SQLCloseCursor`, `SQLFreeStmt(SQL_CLOSE)`, the catalog and `SQLGetTypeInfo` setups, and the rollback that closes cursors. Grep for the caller list; it *is* the invariant, and the comment now says so instead of the opposite. **Tests:** three in `test_cursor_and_data.cpp` next to D37’s - `TheSameCellReadTwiceInTwoResultSetsReadsTheSameValue`, `AnAbandonedChunkedReadDoesNotResumeInTheNextResultSet` and `ACatalogResultSetDoesNotInheritTheLastOnesOffset`. **The third one passed against the unfixed driver at first draft** and had to be rewritten: it asserted only `rc != SQL_NO_DATA`, which cannot see silent truncation, and it compared a SELECT’s column 1 against a catalog column 3, which never collide. It now reads the same catalog cell twice and compares. **Proved:** all three fail with the resets stripped out, and four of D83’s DML tests fail with them. **Reference report byte-identical in both configurations** - so no probe in the tool detects this, which is its own gap: → **D86**. | 03060f9 |
| D83 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Mock + probe | `UPDATE` never applied its SET clause, and no probe read an UPDATEd row back | **Two halves that kept each other alive.** `ParsedQuery` had no field for the SET clause, and the executor’s comment said why: *"the mock has no SET evaluator, and no probe today reads back UPDATEd values."* Both were true, and each excused the other. `test_sqlrowcount_after_update` runs `UPDATE ODBC_TEST_ROUNDTRIP SET VAL = 'X'`, asserts `SQLRowCount == 3` and returns - so **a driver reporting the right count and writing nothing passed the suite clean**, which is the silent-corruption shape this tool exists to find, in the one statement that had no lever for it. **The mock now applies it, through I6’s op log rather than around it.** `WriteOp::Kind::Update` carries the predicate and the resolved cell assignments, so an UPDATE inside a transaction is buffered, rolled back with everything else, and replayed at COMMIT against whatever is committed by then - the three properties Stage A gave INSERT and DELETE, for free, and four tests in `test_transaction_isolation.cpp` that check "for free" was not a guess. `parse_sql_literal` moved out of `make_where_filter`’s body to file scope rather than being copied: the SET parser needs D13’s doubled-quote rule and D12’s "a number only when it is the whole token", and two copies would drift. The clause splits on **top-level commas only**, so `SET NOTE = 'a,b'` is one assignment. A SET naming a column the table has not got is **42S22**, the answer a SELECT of an unknown column gives - silently ignoring it is the one answer not allowed. **D10’s deferral had to end here too.** `substitute_params` left UPDATE alone on purpose while there was no SET evaluator; with one, leaving it alone stops being neutral - `SET v = ?` would write NULL over the cell. UPDATE now numbers its SET markers first and continues into the WHERE, one sequence across the statement. **The probe:** `test_update_applies_its_set_clause`, separate from the row-count one because they grade different claims and fail for different reasons. It updates **one row of three**, so it grades both halves of what UPDATE promises - the matched row changed, the unmatched rows did not - and a driver applying the SET to every row fails the same read. **The failing configuration (AGENTS.md step 6):** `SilentCorruption=DropUpdates`, DropInserts’ sibling one statement over. It resolves the SET targets (so 42S22 still fires) and then writes none of them; **the row count stays honest deliberately**, because that is what makes the loss silent. **Measured:** default → `PASS`, `DropUpdates` → `FAIL`, and under `DropUpdates` `test_sqlrowcount_after_update` **still passes** - not a gap in that probe, which grades SQLRowCount correctly, but the demonstration that grading the count was never going to catch this. All three are asserted by the e2e `AnUpdateThatChangesNothingIsCaughtByReadingItBack`. **Reference number moved, per the guardrail**: **206 → 207**, scored and passed **191 → 192**, still 0 failed, 0 skipped, 100%. The normalised diff is exactly the one added probe and the three summary counts - no existing probe changed, in either configuration. README’s three figures updated. **Tests**: mock 401 → **415** - seven in `test_dml.cpp` (including the comma-in-a-literal and doubled-quote cases, 42S22, and the SET-then-WHERE parameter numbering), four in `test_transaction_isolation.cpp`, three in `test_silent_corruption.cpp`. **Found along the way**: **D85**, a `SQLGetData` continuation outliving its result set - the first read-back test in this row is what caught it, and four of these tests fail without that fix. | e65578f |
| D86 | ✅ DONE | MED | Coverage | No probe detects a `SQLGetData` continuation that outlives its result set (**D85**) | **A fix with no probe behind it.** D85 was a real defect in this repo’s own mock, and the reference report was **byte-identical before and after fixing it**, in both configurations - measured. So a third-party driver shipping the same bug would have passed the suite clean. **The mock had to be able to be that driver again**: `SilentCorruption=StaleGetDataOffset` turns `reset_getdata_continuation()` back into the no-op it effectively was before D85, so the retrieval offset survives its result set. The method moved out of line to `handles.cpp` to consult the config - pulling `BehaviorController` into a header that deliberately forward-declares `TxnBuffer` to keep mock includes out of itself would be the wrong trade for one branch. **The probe**: `test_getdata_restarts_in_a_new_result_set`, next to its sibling `test_getdata_same_column_twice` - same column twice in *one* result set was already graded; across *two* is a different question and had nothing. A literal `SELECT`, with the FROM-less form tried first and the RDB$DATABASE / DUAL variants after it, so the probe needs no DDL and knows the value it should get back. It distinguishes the two failure shapes, and grades the quiet one hardest: once the stale offset is past the end the driver returns `SQL_NO_DATA` into an untouched buffer, but when the new value is longer the offset is a *valid* position, so the call succeeds and silently drops the leading characters. **The first draft was decorative and the failing configuration is what caught it**: it allocated a fresh `OdbcStatement` per read, and the retrieval offset lives on the *statement*, so each read got a clean one and the probe passed against a driver deliberately configured to fail it. One handle, executed twice - which is also what an application does when it loops on a statement. **Measured**: default → `PASS`, `StaleGetDataOffset` → `FAIL`, and under that mode **exactly one probe in 208 fails** - the new one. A mode that broke fetching generally would fail plenty and prove nothing about this probe’s reach, so the e2e scenario `AStaleGetDataOffsetIsCaughtByReadingACellTwice` asserts the blast radius too. **That assertion was wrong on the first push and CI said so**: it was written `EXPECT_EQ(failed, 1)` from a Windows measurement, and Linux and macOS report **4**. Neither number is a property of the mode - any probe that reuses one statement handle across two executes and re-reads a cell observes the stale offset too, and how many do depends on how the driver manager routes `SQLGetData`, so 4 is as correct there as 1 is here. It is now a ceiling (`1 <= failed <= 6`), which keeps what the check was for - a mode failing dozens would say nothing about this probe’s reach - without pinning one platform’s number as universal. Same shape as the `Mode=Success` canary’s `kMaxFailed`, which **§8** loosened for exactly this reason. **Reference number moved, per the guardrail**: **207 → 208**, scored and passed **192 → 193**, still 0 failed, 0 skipped, 100%; the normalised diff is exactly the one added probe, in both configurations. README’s three figures updated. **Mock tests** 425 → **430**, including one that the mode genuinely reproduces D85 and one that the default still does not. | d215f91 |
| D87 | ✅ DONE | MED | Config | `FailOn=SQLPrepareZ` matched nothing and said nothing | **The D26 class, one level down.** D26 made `FailOn` work outside `Mode=Partial`; a *misspelt* name was still accepted in silence - the connection opened, the mock behaved normally, and a fault-injection run quietly exercised the success path with the caller believing it had injected a fault. **What counts as a real name** is the union of two sets: the 46 ANSI implementations that consult `should_fail("<name>")`, and the 31 W wrappers, which push their own `__func__` as a `WEntryScope` for `should_fail` to match (**D78**). All 31 are effective - `SQLBrowseConnectW` reaches a consulting implementation through `SQLDriverConnectW`. **77 names.** An entry point that never consults `should_fail` is *not* in the set and is rejected like a typo, because naming one is exactly as silent (`SQLBrowseConnect`, the ANSI one, is the live example). **Diagnosed, not refused**: an `01000` record naming the offending entries and `SQL_SUCCESS_WITH_INFO`, which is what a driver does with a connection-string key it cannot honour. `SQL_ERROR` would punish a caller who misspelt one name of five and wanted the other four, and the connection is not the thing that is wrong - the entries that are right keep working. **The list cannot drift**: `src/driver/fail_on_names.hpp` is generated by `mock-driver/tools/check_fail_on_names.py`, and the lint job re-derives the set from the sources and fails on any difference - same role as the `mockodbc.def` drift check (**F11**). Without it a new `should_fail` call site would have its own name rejected. **Tests:** six in `test_config.cpp` (unknown, known, W names, case-insensitivity, one-bad-name-in-a-list, and the never-consulted entry point) and two in `test_fault_injection_reach.cpp` that go through `SQLDriverConnect` and `SQLGetDiagRec`, where an application would look - because recording the name inside `DriverConfig` and not reporting it is the same bug one field further out. | 6adc94b |
| D88 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Dead code | `should_fail`’s `BehaviorMode::Partial` case could never match | The case re-scanned `fail_on` for the function name. It could not match: the block at the top of `should_fail` returns for **every** non-empty `fail_on` - `true` on a hit, and `false` on a miss when the mode is `Partial` - so control only reaches the switch with `fail_on` **empty**, and the loop iterated over nothing before returning false. Replaced by the `return false` it always was, with the reasoning written down. **Tests:** `PartialWithoutAFailOnListFailsNothing` and `PartialWithAFailOnListStillNarrowsToIt` pin both halves, so the behaviour is asserted rather than merely believed unchanged. | 6adc94b |
| D89 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Dead code | `BehaviorController::should_fail` had zero callers | Every site asks the `DriverConfig` it already holds - `BehaviorController::instance().config().should_fail(...)` - because `config()` returns a copy under the mutex and the call site needs the rest of that copy anyway (`error_code`, `apply_latency`). The forwarder took the mutex a second time to answer a question nobody asked it. Removed; the header says why, so it is not helpfully re-added. | 6adc94b |
| D90 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Mock | The driver wrote to `stderr` on every parameterised `SELECT` | **Found while reading `substitute_params` for D83.** `fprintf(stderr, "[T] before='%s'")` and its `after` twin, committed in `b2afc49` on the **D10** WHERE-marker path, fired on every parameterised `SELECT` or `DELETE` whose `WHERE` holds a `?` - unconditional, unguarded, from inside a shared library loaded into someone else’s process. Two lines that no compiler flag and no test could object to. Removed. **Test:** a rule in `tools/check_source_text.py` - no `printf`/`fprintf(stderr)`/`fprintf(stdout)`/`std::cout`/`puts` anywhere under `mock-driver/src`. A lint rule rather than a gtest, because `stderr` is a process-global a test would have to capture and restore, while the property wanted is that the call is not in the source. Scoped to the driver: the mock’s own tests print, `tools/` are command-line programs, and the crusher is an application whose job is to write a report. **Proved:** reinstating one `fprintf` makes it name `param_binding.cpp:400`. | 6adc94b |
| D91 | ✅ DONE | MED | Mock | Freeing a connected environment leaks the catalog’s `attach` refcount | **The row is half right, and the half that is wrong is the half that matters.** True: `MockCatalog::detach()` has exactly one caller, `SQLDisconnect`, and `~ConnectionHandle` does not call it. False: that an application can reach that destructor with a connected handle. **Both `delete` sites are guarded** - `SQLFreeHandle(SQL_HANDLE_DBC)` refuses with **HY010** while `is_connected()`, so a connection reaching its destructor has already been through `SQLDisconnect`, which detaches; and `SQLFreeHandle(SQL_HANDLE_ENV)` refuses with **HY010** while `connections_` is non-empty, so the `delete env` that would run `~EnvironmentHandle`’s child-deleting loop is unreachable while any connection exists at all, connected or not. (**D8(a)**’s fix to that loop is still right and still needed - it is reached by the mock’s own tests, which construct handles directly.) **So the leak is theoretical, and those two refusals are the entire reason.** Neither had a test, which is the real gap and the one that got closed: `FreeingAConnectionThatIsStillOpenIsRefused`, `FreeingAnEnvironmentWithAllocatedConnectionsIsRefused` (both asserting HY010, and the second checking the refusal survives a `SQLDisconnect` - the guard is on the list being non-empty, not on the connection being open), and `AConnectCycleIsRefcountNeutral`, which pins the accounting itself against `live_connections()`. **Proved**: relaxing the `connections_.empty()` check makes the second fail and the other five `CatalogSharing` tests pass, so it is that guard being tested and not something incidental. **No defensive `detach()` was added to the destructor**, deliberately: it would be unreachable code guarding an invariant the guards already hold, it could double-release if that invariant ever changed, and a protection nobody can exercise has not been shown to protect anything - this project’s own standard, applied to itself. If either guard is ever relaxed the leak becomes real, and now a test fails first and says so. | d215f91 |
| D92 | ✅ DONE | MED | Probes | The persistence probes could not observe the commit they issue | **They ran in autocommit ON**, where `SQLEndTran(SQL_COMMIT)` is a no-op the driver is entitled to ignore - every row was already committed by the `INSERT` that made it. So `commit_now()` reported success whatever the driver would have done with a real commit, and all of **A22**’s attribution machinery had nothing to attribute. Noted while planning **I6** for two probes; it was the whole §1.1 round-trip matrix, which shares two helpers. **Measured before fixing**: under `Mode=Partial;FailOn=SQLEndTran` all three probes `FailedCommitIsBlamedOnTheCommitNotTheBindPath` watches **PASSed**, so its `reached` counter was **0 of 3** and every assertion in its loop was dead code - on every platform, not just some. The one probe in the file that does open a transaction, `test_param_bind_once_execute_many_endtran`, failed correctly, which is what made the contrast visible. **Fixed** with `ScopedAutocommitOff`, the mirror of `ScopedAutocommitOn`: it saves what the connection actually had, opens the transaction, and restores - and it is declared outside the `try` so it covers the commit and is released before the `DROP`, since DDL inside an open transaction is what **A14** exists to avoid. Applied to both round-trip helpers and to the two probes that carry their own INSERT loops. **A driver that declines `SQL_AUTOCOMMIT_OFF` is not failed for it** - `engaged()` is false, the probe carries on, and the report says the commit was a no-op and is not graded, because this probe tests the bind path. **The e2e scenario stops tolerating a PASS**: `if (status == "PASS") continue;` was written for the broken state and, with the commit now genuinely forwarded, a PASS is a real disagreement. The `reached` counter went with it - a number whose honest value was always 0 made the silence look like a platform difference. **Measured after**: all three `FAIL`, name `SQLEndTran(SQL_COMMIT) rc=-1 [40001]`, and blame the commit rather than the Firebird #161 bind path. **Default reference report byte-identical** in both configurations - when the commit works, the rows land and every probe still passes. | 4f067c3 |
| D93 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | E2E harness | The harness discarded the result of every temp-file delete, and **D80 closed on the wrong cause** | `slurp_and_remove` ran `fs::remove(p, ec)` and dropped `ec` on the floor. On Windows a delete is refused while any handle to the file is open, and `TerminateProcess` returns **before** the kernel has finished closing the handles of the process it killed - so the delete was refused, nothing retried, and the file stayed in the temp directory for good. **`AWedgedCrusherIsKilledRatherThanWaitedOn` has been reporting this correctly all along.** **D80 looked here and found the other bug**: the assertion counted *every* `crusher_e2e_` file in a shared directory, so a sibling run failed it. That was real and the before/after snapshot fixed it - but the row then recorded "verified 3/3 back-to-back (**and that was not enough evidence** - the test still failed about half the time for a second, unrelated reason in the harness itself: see **D93**)" and stopped, and three consecutive passes is not evidence about a fault that fires half the time. **Measured today under `ctest`: 3 failures in 6 at `e65578f`**, and 1 in 5 on a working tree - always the same assertion, always exactly one orphaned `.stderr.log`. **The first fix attempt was wrong and is worth recording**: a 2-second poll *in the test*, on the theory that the handle was closing. It changed nothing (3 failures in 8) - which is the evidence that the file is never released, because nobody ever asks again. The assertion was never the thing at fault. **Fixed in the harness**, where the leak is: `remove_with_retry` retries for a second and **reports** into the stderr the caller is about to read when it runs out, because a harness that cannot delete its own temp file is describing a child that outlived its kill - a finding, not housekeeping. Applied to the `.json` temp files too, which had the identical exposure at four sites. **A second is far short of the bug this guards**: the first cut of the **D55** fix left `odbc-crusher` running with `Latency=30s`, holding its stderr file for thirty seconds - still a failure here by a factor of thirty. **Proved:** 10 runs in 10 green after, against 3-in-6 failing before. | 67e70c2 |

## Part E — Build system and static analysis

| ID | Status | Sev | Area | Task | Details / Evidence | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Warnings | Apply `project_warnings` to every target — it currently covers 3 of 8 | Linked `project_warnings` + `project_options` from the five targets that lacked them: `odbc_crusher_reporting`, the `odbc-crusher` executable, `odbc_crusher_tests`, `odbc_crusher_e2e_tests` (root project) and both mock targets. The mock is a separate CMake project, so rather than duplicating the flag list it now `include()`s the parent's `cmake/CompilerWarnings.cmake` via a relative module path, guarded by `if(NOT TARGET project_warnings)` and an `EXISTS` check that degrades to empty interface libraries with a `message(WARNING)` if `mock-driver/` is ever copied out of the repo. **Warning count corrected**: the plan predicted 3 app + 6 mock; the actual count was 3 app + 11 mock across 7 distinct sites — the six `[](unsigned char c){ return std::toupper(c); }` lambdas (`config.cpp:15`, `mock_catalog.cpp:13,33`, `mock_data.cpp:88,766`, `info_api.cpp:21`) each return `int` into a `char` and were invisible before because MSVC reports them at `<algorithm>:3800`, not at the call site. All fixed, `/WX` kept. Verified: both projects rebuild with zero warnings; 107/107 mock, 27/27 unit, 14/14 e2e, 195/195 against the mock. **Regression test**: `/WX` on MSVC *is* the test — any reintroduced narrowing fails the build. Note that the GCC/Clang branch of `CompilerWarnings.cmake` carries no `-Werror` (pre-existing design), so on Linux/macOS these flags warn but do not gate; see the new task **E5**. | da4e33a |
| E2 | ✅ DONE | MED | Build hygiene | Stop generating `version.hpp` into the source tree | `cmake/Version.cmake` now `configure_file()`s into `${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/generated/odbc_crusher/version.hpp` and exposes it through a new `odbc_crusher_version` INTERFACE target, linked by the two targets that actually include the header (`odbc-crusher` and `odbc_crusher_reporting`) — target-based rather than a global `include_directories()`, per AGENTS.md. Deleted the stale `include/odbc_crusher/version.hpp` from the working tree and dropped the `.gitignore` entry that hid it, leaving a comment there so nobody re-adds the entry together with a source-tree `configure_file()`. Verified with a from-scratch configure: the header lands in `build/generated/`, `--version` still reports `0.5.0` from the git tag, and `git status --untracked-files=all` shows nothing in `include/`. **Test**: a new `ci.yml` step, "Check the build did not write into the source tree", runs `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` after configure + build and fails on any output — so any *future* generated artifact that lands in the source tree fails CI instead of being papered over with another `.gitignore` line. This is a stronger check than the task asked for, and it is why the `.gitignore` entry had to go rather than merely being left harmless. | 1d668eb |
| E3 | ✅ DONE | MED | Build | Deduplicate the GoogleTest dependency | Picked "hoist the pin into `cmake/Dependencies.cmake`" over "make the mock a subdirectory of the main build". Reasoning: the mock is a standalone CMake project deliberately — `cmake -S mock-driver` is what `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, the register scripts and the docs all use, and it is built and shipped as its own artifact on all three platforms before the app is built — so folding it into the root build would couple two things that are separately released. The duplicate `_deps` tree is a cost; the version *drift* was the correctness problem, and that is what is fixed. `cmake/Dependencies.cmake` now holds all three pins (googletest v1.17.0, CLI11 v2.6.2, nlohmann/json v3.12.0) plus an `odbc_crusher_fetch_googletest()` macro used by both projects. **Diverged from the task text in one way**: rather than giving the mock a fallback pin for the case where `../cmake` is missing, the mock now hard-`FATAL_ERROR`s. A fallback would have meant a second literal `GIT_TAG v1.17.0` in the tree with a "keep in sync" comment — precisely the drift being removed. Nothing builds `mock-driver/` out of tree (checked `ci.yml` and `release.yml`), so the requirement costs nothing. **Test**: `grep -c googletest.git` over `cmake/Dependencies.cmake`, `CMakeLists.txt` and `mock-driver/CMakeLists.txt` returns 1/0/0 — there is exactly one pin, so drift is structurally impossible rather than merely discouraged; and the mock's `FATAL_ERROR` means a copy that tried to reintroduce one would not configure. Also updated both `actions/cache` keys in `ci.yml` to hash `cmake/Dependencies.cmake`, which is now where a bump happens — without that, a version bump would have silently reused a stale `_deps` cache. | 1d668eb |
| E5 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Warnings | The warning interface has no `-Werror` on GCC/Clang, so it only gates MSVC | **Enforced now, and proven**: run **34193192700** is green on all 14 jobs with `-Werror` in effect, and the Linux app, Linux mock and macOS mock builds report **0 compiler warnings**. That the flag is real is not an inference - five successive runs failed on `[-Werror=...]`-attributed diagnostics before this one passed. (macOS still emits two `ld` warnings about duplicate libraries in the link line; those are link-time, outside `-Werror`, and benign - noted rather than claimed as zero.) **Followed this row's own recommendation**: narrow the set so the promise matches what is enforced, then gate. Dropped exactly the three the measurement found in bulk - 1,447 of 1,636: `-Wold-style-cast` (780, almost all `(SQLCHAR*)"literal"` at ODBC call sites), `-Wsign-conversion` (667, `SQLSMALLINT`/`SQLULEN`/`size_t` traffic inherent to the API), `-Wuseless-cast` (93). A warning nobody can act on is noise, and the noise is what let the rest hide. **`AGENTS.md`'s "compiles without warnings on Windows *and Linux*" is now true.** **Two things the flag surfaced that were not style.** `param_binding.cpp` wrote dates into buffers sized for plausible input rather than possible input: `month`/`day` are `SQLUSMALLINT` and a bound parameter may hold 65535 in either - five digits each - while `year` prints six and `%09u` is a floor, not a ceiling. The 16-byte date buffer needed 19 and the 40-byte timestamp 48. `snprintf` truncates rather than overflows, so it was silently wrong rather than unsafe, which for a conformance fixture is the worse of the two. And `apply_silent_corruption`'s switch silently swallowed four `SilentCorruption` modes. **Six pieces of dead code**, each of which had a reason to look alive: three copies of `first_sqlstate` whose call sites resolve to `TestBase`'s member instead (`boundary_tests.cpp` says "a stand-in that C5 deletes" - C5 deleted the others); `hex_bytes`, orphaned by my own **D64**; `escape_sequence_tests.cpp`'s `match` lambda and its `total_passed` counter, sitting between two counters that *are* read (**C6**); and a `overall_start`/`overall_end`/`total` trio dead since **G6**. **Three mistakes of mine on the way, recorded because the record is the point.** (1) The row was marked DONE at `4f94b3c` in a commit that did not contain the change - I wrote the CMake edit as a script and ran only the follow-up script. The green CI that followed proved nothing had changed; found by grepping a build log for the flag and seeing 91 warnings from three flags that should have been gone. (2) A watcher that took "the most recent run" raced a push and reported a previous commit's failures as current; it matches on `headSha` now. (3) I cast `overall_end` to void and left `overall_start` feeding it, which moved the warning to the other half of a dead pair rather than removing it. **Two portability findings worth keeping.** Clang folds `-Wsign-conversion` into `-Wconversion` while GCC keeps them separate, so macOS silently re-enabled the 667 this set drops - and only macOS; `-Wno-sign-conversion` after `-Wconversion` makes the decision mean the same thing on both. And `-Werror` turned two hits inside vendored `nlohmann/json.hpp` into build failures: fetched dependencies now get `INTERFACE_SYSTEM_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES`, applied to every fetched target rather than the one that tripped first, so our warning set is a statement about our code rather than an opinion about a dependency's. `FetchContent`'s own `SYSTEM` option needs CMake 3.25 and this project requires 3.20. | ea353eb |
| E4 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Tooling | Make the documented `clang-tidy` invocation actually work | **Already fixed, and the row's evidence is stale** - `CONTRIBUTING.md:585-587` refers to a version of a file that is now 103 lines. The Static Analysis section already reads `cmake -B build-tidy -G Ninja -DCMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS=ON` followed by `clang-tidy -p build-tidy ...`, which is exactly the Ninja configure the row asks for; it was rewritten by the documentation work after this row was written. **Confirmed rather than assumed**, since "already fixed" is the claim Part 0 exists to distrust: ran it. The Visual Studio tree has no `compile_commands.json`, as the row says; the documented Ninja configure produces one with **61 entries**. (`clang-tidy` itself is not installed on this host, so the invocation past the compile database is unverified - the part E4 is about, the database, is verified.) **One real gap found and closed while checking**: `build-tidy/` was not in `.gitignore`, so a contributor following the documentation was left with an untracked build tree. ## Part F — CI/CD, release, and stress-test

| ID | Status | Sev | Area | Task | Details / Evidence | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | ✅ DONE | MED | Provenance | Verify the apt pins that have never run (carried from §7.1, restated) | **Dispatched** (`gh workflow run stress-test.yml -f driver=all`, run **34187930345**) - the first full run since 2026-04-27, and the first to exercise anything but firebird since 2026-02-09. **All seven jobs green.** **The pins resolve exactly.** `Setting up odbc-postgresql:amd64 (1:16.00.0000-1)` and `Setting up odbc-mariadb (3.1.15-3)`, matching the manifest strings character for character. The concern behind this row - that Ubuntu had dropped a pinned version out from under a workflow nobody had run in months - was unfounded, and now it is known rather than assumed. **Results** (the composite pulls the binary from the latest successful master CI run, not the last release, so these are current code): PostgreSQL 169/194 scored, **87.1%**, 10 failed + 1 error · MariaDB 130/194, 67.0%, 11 failed · MySQL 112/194, 57.7%, 18 failed · DuckDB 94/139, 67.6%, 25 failed + 8 errors · ClickHouse 136/194, 70.1%, 38 failed + 5 errors · **Mock 193/193, 100.0%** - identical to the local reference run, which is the cross-check that the CI binary and this workspace agree. **Firebird is the finding.** The driver **segfaults**: `[ERR!] Descriptor Tests (DRIVER CRASH) — Segmentation fault (SIGSEGV)`. The crash guard caught it and carried on, exactly as designed, and then the process wedged and was killed at the 570-second timeout (`exit code 137`). The job reported **success** anyway - see **F12**, which this uncovered. | 7fa5253 |
| F2 | ✅ DONE | MED | Robustness | Make a timed-out run still produce a JSON report | Chose "stream to the file as categories complete" over "a per-category watchdog inside crusher". Reasoning: CI escalates to `SIGKILL` at 600s, which no in-process watchdog can survive, and interrupting a wedged `SQLExecute` would need `SQLCancel` from a second thread — which a hang-prone driver is precisely the one not to honour. Streaming also restores parity with the text run, which already survives via `tee`. `JsonReporter::write_snapshot()` writes to `<file>.partial` and `std::filesystem::rename`s over the target, so a signal landing mid-write can never leave a truncated document; the report carries a new top-level `complete` boolean (`false` mid-run, `true` in the final write) so a consumer can tell a killed run's output from a finished one. Additive, so `schema_version` stays 1. **Measured, and then corrected, a performance regression I introduced**: rewriting the whole ~100 KB document after each of 23 categories cost ~150 ms per run and took the e2e suite from 5.6s to 10.0s. Making intermediate snapshots compact rather than indented barely helped (the cost is the file write plus rename, not serialisation), so snapshots are now rate-limited to one per second, with the first always written. e2e is back to 5.4s and the mock run to ~126 ms, while a hang — which by definition lasts far longer than a second, against a 570s cap — still leaves everything that completed. **Test**: new `tests/unit/test_json_reporter.cpp`, 6 cases driving the reporter directly, because the interesting state is the mid-run one and a subprocess test would have to race the kill against the write. It asserts a usable, `complete:false`, header-bearing report exists on disk *before* `report_end()` is ever called; that the final report is marked complete and carries the summary; that no `.partial` is left behind; that the rate limiter both suppresses and later releases snapshots; that stdout mode writes no file; and that an unwritable path neither throws nor emits one error line per category. README documents the `complete` key. | 31eb8a6 |
| F3 | ✅ DONE | MED | Release | Run the test suites in `release.yml` | `ctest -L unit` now runs in the `build` job before the smoke test, and the whole mock suite runs in `build-mock-driver` before packaging - that job used to publish a driver it had never once executed, and the mock is an artifact people register on their own machines. **Chose `ctest` over `needs:` the CI workflow**, which is the row's other suggestion and does not work: a tag push is its own event, there is no `ci.yml` run to depend on, and `workflow_run` cannot gate a job on a different trigger's result. Running the suite is also stricter - it tests the commit being packaged rather than some build of it that happened elsewhere. **Unit only in the tool's job**, deliberately: the e2e suite needs a registered mock driver, a per-platform install this job has no reason to do, and `ci.yml` covers it on every push. This is the last gate before an artifact is published, not a second CI. | ef3cc8d |
| F4 | ✅ DONE | LOW | CI hygiene | Remove leftover debug output from the composite action | Gone. The raw `gh run list` dump printed on every stress-test job, from diagnostic commit `ddcf3cd`; the parsing bug it was investigating was fixed in `e35a8a7` and the noise stayed. The error branch below it survives - "no successful master CI run found, wait for a green ci.yml" is the part that was ever useful, and it is the case where output actually helps. | 20308a8 |
| F5 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Duplication | Resolve `fetch-stress-test.ps1` (carried from §7.3) | **Refactored to read the manifest** - option (a). The `$Drivers` hashtable is gone; the script builds its driver list from `.github/drivers.json`, skipping any entry whose `source.repo` is `self`, and throws if the manifest is missing or yields nothing. Verified by running the block: **6 drivers, firebird included** - the hashtable had 5 and was missing firebird entirely, so the drift the row called "real" was real and would have silently skipped the driver this project cares most about. **Why (a) and not delete.** The row names the defect as "the duplicated source-of-truth", and the refactor removes exactly that. README documents this script as the path for someone without the `/triage-driver` skill, so deleting it trades away a documented workflow to fix a data problem. And deleting a file I did not write is the kind of thing to ask about rather than assume - the standing instruction lists it. **One claim in the row is wrong and is corrected rather than quietly skipped**: it says the script writes to a `recommendations/` directory that no longer exists. Line 177 calls `Ensure-Directory` on it, so it is created on demand; nothing to fix. | 20308a8 |
| F6 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Security | Add `permissions:` blocks to `ci.yml` and `stress-test.yml` | `ci.yml` gets `contents: read` - nothing in it writes to the repository, and `actions/upload-artifact` does not use this token. `stress-test.yml` gets `contents: read` plus `actions: read`, which is what `gh run download` needs to resolve the CI run and fetch its artifact, and nothing more. Both previously inherited the repository default, i.e. whatever the org setting happens to be on any given day. | 20308a8 |
| F7 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Supply chain | Stop executing an unpinned remote script in CI | `bash <(curl .../actionlint/main/scripts/download-actionlint.bash)` ran whatever was on that branch that day, on every push. It is also the **first** thing that runs - the lint job gates the rest of the matrix - so a compromised script would have seen the whole workflow before anything else did. Now pinned to release `v1.7.12` and verified: `sha256sum -c -` against `8aca8db96f1b94770f1b0d72b6dddcb1ebb8123cb3712530b08cc387b349a3d8`, which was computed by downloading the asset. A moved tag or a swapped asset fails the job instead of executing. `set -euo pipefail` so a failed download cannot fall through to a stale binary. | 20308a8 |
| F8 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Manifest | Fix the dead `mock-driver.install.url` | The manifest said `method: release_asset` with a `releases/latest` URL while `stress-test.yml` has always downloaded `mock-odbc-driver-Windows-Release` from the most recent successful master `ci.yml` run. It now describes what actually happens - `method: ci_artifact`, with the workflow and artifact names - and says *why*, because using CI rather than the release is deliberate: it closes the release-cadence gap so a stress run exercises the driver as it is now, not as it was at the last tag. The release URL is kept under `release_asset_url` rather than deleted: it exists and is what a **user** installs; it is simply not what CI uses. The related note - `release.yml` ships the mock for Windows only while `ci.yml` builds it on all three platforms - is recorded there for whoever picks it up. Edited as text rather than re-serialised, because a `json.dumps` round-trip reformatted the whole file and buried three real lines in sixteen. | 20308a8 |
| F9 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Coverage | Run the e2e suite in the Debug matrix slots too | The `matrix.build_type == 'Release'` gate is gone from `Download Mock Driver` and both e2e steps, and the two `ctest` calls follow the matrix instead of hard-coding `-C Release`. Half the six-slot matrix used to build the binary and never run it against anything. **The mock artifact stays Release** while the tool is Debug. That is not a compromise - it is the realistic pairing, since a driver under test is always somebody else's release build - and the ODBC boundary is a C API, so the mixed runtime is not a problem. **Verified locally before pushing**: `ctest -C Debug -L e2e` against the registered Release mock, 38/38. The alternative was finding out in CI whether a Debug tool can drive a Release driver on Windows. Left Release-only, correctly: `Report the reference-run baseline` (a measurement, and one number per platform is the point) and the mock job's export-drift check (a property of the built DLL). | ef3cc8d |
| F10 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Portability | `timeout(1)` is absent on macOS runners | The composite action had a `macOS)` branch for artifact selection and then called GNU `timeout` unconditionally, so it advertised support it did not have. A `run_with_timeout` helper now prefers `timeout`, falls back to `gtimeout` (coreutils via Homebrew), and **fails loudly if neither exists** rather than running the driver unbounded - the 570s limit is what stops a wedged driver holding a runner for six hours, so silently dropping it would be worse than the original bug. Both call sites use it. Latent today (no macOS stress-test job) and cheaper to fix than to rediscover when someone adds one. | 20308a8 |
| F12 | ✅ DONE | MED | CI | A killed crusher run reports the stress-test job as success | Found by **F1**'s dispatch. The Firebird job is green, and inside it the driver segfaults, the crash guard reports `Descriptor Tests (DRIVER CRASH)`, the process then wedges, and `##[error]Process completed with exit code 137` - killed at the timeout. `continue-on-error: true` turned all of that into a green tick. The **D51** shape again: the gate goes quiet at the moment it has something to say. **The blanket setting existed for a real reason and G3 removed it.** crusher exited 1 for any FAIL or ERROR, so against a real driver the code was always 1 and the job could never be green - `continue-on-error` was the only way, and it swallowed killed runs with everything else. `--fail-on=none` now makes probe results not affect the exit code, so a non-zero exit means the **tool** did not finish: killed (124/137), could not connect (2), or usage (3). **Verified the contract this depends on** rather than assuming it: `--fail-on=none` against `BufferValidation=Lenient` (a real FAIL) exits **0**, and against `Mode=Failure` (no connection) exits **2**. Without that second half the whole change would mask connection failures instead of revealing hangs. `continue-on-error` stays, so one wedged driver does not stop the other six reporting, but the exit code is now inspected and turned into a `::warning::` naming what happened - visible in the run summary rather than only in the log. `PIPESTATUS[0]` on the text run, because the pipe into `tee` would otherwise report tee's status, which is always 0. **No automated test**: this is a property of a GitHub composite action running against real drivers, and the only way to exercise it is to dispatch a stress-test run - which is what will confirm it. Stated rather than left implied. | 7fa5253 |
| F13 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Release | A build from the source tarball reported version `0.1.0`, silently | **Found by checking what a release actually ships before tagging v0.7.0.** Every GitHub release attaches "Source code (tar.gz)", and that archive has no `.git`. `cmake/Version.cmake` reads the version from `git describe` inside `if(GIT_FOUND AND EXISTS "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/.git")`, and when that guard fails the whole block is skipped **with no message at all**, falling back to the `project()` version - which had been **0.1.0 since the first commit**. **Measured, not inferred**: `git archive HEAD` into a clean directory, then configure, and CMake prints `ODBC Crusher version: 0.1.0`. Anyone building the published archive got a binary claiming a version from before almost all of this work. **The existing guard could not catch it.** `release.yml` already verifies the built binary’s `--version` contains the tag - but that check runs on a `fetch-depth: 0` checkout, where `git describe` succeeds and the `project()` fallback is never consulted. It is correct exactly when the broken path is not taken. **Fixed in three places, because any one alone leaves a hole:** the fallback is now `0.7.0`, so a tarball build is right rather than wrong; the absent-`.git` branch emits a `message(WARNING)` naming where the version came from, so a packager who bumps nothing is still told - the two warnings that already existed both sit *inside* the `if` and cover cases that only arise inside a repository, while the one case that reaches people who never cloned it was the silent one, the **D26** class again; and `release.yml` now refuses to publish when `project()` disagrees with the tag, so the next release cannot quietly ship a stale fallback. **Proved**: an archive of the tree with no `.git` configures to `ODBC Crusher version: 0.7.0` and prints the new warning, where before it printed `0.1.0` and nothing. | 3a2fecc |
| F11 | ✅ DONE | MED | CI correctness | Make the `.def` drift check bidirectional | `ci.yml`'s "Check mockodbc.def export drift" step is now bidirectional and rests on a new sanctioned-exceptions file, `mock-driver/src/mockodbc.not-exported.txt`, holding the 38 symbols that are implemented but deliberately absent from the `.def` (the 7 ODBC 2.x aliases and the 31 ANSI entry points the W exports delegate to), each family with its rationale. Four assertions, all four proven to trip locally against doctored inputs: (1) `.def → source`, an export with no implementation — trips on a bogus `SQLNeverImplemented`; (2) **`source → .def`**, the direction that was missing and the reason **D2** went unnoticed — trips with `UNLISTED: SQLGetDiagRec` when that name is removed from the allow-list; (3) a name both exported and listed internal — trips on `SQLFetch`; (4) a listed name no longer implemented, so the allow-list cannot go stale — trips on `SQLLongGone`. Also replaced the extraction regex: the old `'^\s{4}SQL[A-Za-z0-9]+\s*$'` silently matched **zero** symbols against a `.def` reindented to 8 spaces, where the new comment-stripping/trimming parser still finds all 58 — verified. A `$exports.Count -lt 50` guard fails loudly if the extraction itself ever breaks, rather than passing vacuously. Current state: 58 exported + 38 deliberately internal = 96 implemented, consistent. | 3b4afbd |

## Part G — CLI and output contract

| ID | Status | Sev | Area | Task | Details / Evidence | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Output | `-o json` to stdout emits non-JSON — the documented pipe usage does not work | Chose **stderr** over "suppress in json mode" (the task offered both): stdout is the data channel and stderr the progress channel is the convention every consumer already expects, it keeps the progress line visible in console mode, and it gives **G2**/**G3** a rule to follow rather than a per-format special case. Two sites moved: `main.cpp`'s `"Phase 2: Running ODBC tests..."` and `json_reporter.cpp`'s `"JSON report written to: …"` — the latter so that `-o json -f report.json` leaves stdout completely empty. **Verified**: `odbc-crusher "…" -o json \| python -c "json.load(sys.stdin)"` now parses; it previously died on `Expecting value: line 1 column 1`. **Test**: new e2e scenario `JsonToStdoutIsParseableWithNoProgressChatter`, backed by a new harness entry point `run_crusher_stdout()` that runs `-o json` with **no** `-f` and captures stdout to a file. It asserts the first non-whitespace byte of stdout is `{` (so a failure names the offending chatter rather than just reporting a parse error), that the document parses, and that the progress line is still present on stderr — so the fix cannot regress into deleting the output instead of rerouting it. README's JSON Output section now documents the stream split. | be455bd |
| G2 | ✅ DONE | MED | Usability | Add a category/test filter and `--list-tests` | `--category` (repeatable, case-insensitive) and `--list-categories`. The harness's workaround comment - *"just ConnectionTests category isn't filterable from CLI, so we live with a full run"* - is now false, and bisecting a driver that hangs no longer means bisecting the source. `--list-categories` needs no connection string, because making someone pass a dummy one to read a list is the kind of small rudeness that stops a tool being adopted. **Individual probes are deliberately not filterable**, and the row's `--list-tests` is answered with `--list-categories` instead: a probe's name is produced by *running* it, so there is nothing to list before a run and nothing saved by discarding results after one. Category is the granularity that exists. **A filtered report says so.** `categories_available` is always published and `categories_selected` appears only when a filter was applied, so a consumer comparing pass rates cannot mistake a filter for a regression. Purely additive, so `schema_version` stays 1 per **G4**'s rule. A `--category` that matches nothing exits 3 and lists what it does know, rather than producing a clean, empty and entirely meaningless report - which in someone's CI would read as "everything passed". **The mistake, and the test that caught it.** The first cut paired each factory with a hand-written display name and **16 of the 23 disagreed** with `category_name()` - `Metadata Tests` against `Metadata/Catalog Tests`, `Descriptors` against `Descriptor Tests` - so `--list-categories` advertised names `--category` would refuse. Exactly the drift a registry is for, introduced by the registry. It holds factories only now, and names come from the instance. `ListedCategoriesAreExactlyTheOnesTheReportUses` compares the two lists and is what found it. **Regression**: five e2e scenarios - the list/report agreement, the filter, case-insensitivity, the filtered-report marker, and the unmatched-category error. | dfa3910 |
| G3 | ✅ DONE | MED | CI adoption | Add `--fail-on=<severity>` | `--fail-on=critical\|error\|warning\|info\|none`. `main.cpp` exited 1 for any FAIL or ERR whatever its severity, so against a real driver the exit code was always 1 - which is why the stress-test composite sets `continue-on-error` and nobody reads it. A driver project can now gate on "no CRITICAL regressions" and mean it. **The default is `info`, the least severe level, so the exit code is byte-for-byte what it always was.** This is opt-in; nothing changes for an existing caller. Two properties worth stating because they are what make it trustworthy: the threshold changes the **exit code only** - every probe still runs and the report is unchanged, since reporting less would be a different tool - and a **connection failure still exits non-zero at every threshold, `none` included**, because there were no probe results to grade and a driver that will not connect is not a passing run. **Regression**: `FailOnSeverityDecidesTheExitCode` reads the worst severity actually present in the report and checks the thresholds around it - every level at or below it must trip, the level above it must not, `none` never does, and the report is unchanged in every case. **It did not start that way, and CI caught it on all three POSIX jobs**: the first version asserted that `BufferValidation=Lenient` produces exactly one FAIL at severity ERROR and that `--fail-on=critical` therefore exits 0. Both hold on Windows and neither holds on POSIX, where the driver managers' buffer overrun (**D1**, **D59**) adds two more failures and one of them is CRITICAL - so `--fail-on=critical` correctly exited non-zero and the test called that a bug. The same shape as **D53**: a scenario asserting a platform's behaviour as though it were the tool's. **D63**'s `report_outline()` printed the summary and the category list straight into the failure, which is how it was one round trip instead of three. `FailOnDoesNotMaskAConnectionFailure` covers the `Mode=Failure` case. Documented in README.md with an exit-code table. | dfa3910 |
| G4 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Output contract | Version the JSON schema and use ISO-8601 timestamps | Added `schema_version` and converted `timestamp` to ISO-8601 UTC. Chose an **integer** version (`1`) rather than a `major.minor` string, matching the convention `.github/drivers.json` already uses in this repo; the semantics are documented on `JsonReporter::kSchemaVersion` and in README: bump when an existing key changes meaning, is renamed or is removed, keep it for purely additive changes, and consumers should reject a version they do not know. `timestamp` was a raw `std::time(nullptr)` integer that every consumer had to know was epoch seconds; it is now `YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm:ssZ` via a `gmtime_s`/`gmtime_r` + `strftime` helper. Grepped for consumers first — nothing in `tests/`, `.claude/skills/`, `.github/` or `fetch-stress-test.ps1` read `timestamp`, so the change breaks nothing. **Test**: new e2e scenario `ReportCarriesSchemaVersionAndIso8601Timestamp` asserts `schema_version` is the integer 1 and walks the timestamp character by character against the exact 20-character layout — hand-rolled rather than `<regex>` so that a 19- or 21-character variant cannot slip past. | be455bd |
| G6 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Output contract | Keep raw timings out of the `actual` field so two reports diff cleanly | Both offending `actual` strings rewritten to carry verdict-relevant facts instead of raw measurements. `test_rapid_cursor_lifecycle` now reports `"100/100 cycles completed"` — the per-probe timing is already in `duration_us`, so repeating it in prose bought nothing and cost report stability. `test_open_close_hammer_loop` reports `"500/500 cycles \| close/open ratio within the 10x threshold"` on the PASS path; the measured ratio is kept **only** on the FAIL path, where the number is the evidence and a diff difference is meaningful. `test_handle_reuse_no_leak`'s count is a small integer that does not vary between runs, so it stays. **Test**: new e2e scenario `TwoRunsDifferOnlyInTimings` runs the binary twice against the same driver, flattens both reports to leaf paths, and asserts that nothing outside `timestamp` and `*duration_us` differs — naming any offender with both values. This makes the Phase 0 exit criterion executable rather than a hand check. **Proved it catches the regression**: reverting `cursor_stress_tests.cpp` makes it fail with exactly the two leaves and their differing values (`/categories/22/tests/0/actual: "…completed in 6036 us (60 us/iteration)" vs "…4877 us (48 us/iteration)"` and `/categories/22/tests/2/actual: "…open_mean=32us close_mean=7us ratio=0.21875" vs "…open_mean=35us close_mean=7us ratio=0.2"`), out of 2,024 leaves. | f53a720 |
| G5 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Reporting | Fix the console reporter's warnings and duplicate storage | Split across two commits, both halves done. The **warnings** half landed with **E1** (`da4e33a`): `console_reporter.cpp:9`'s unused `connection_string` is now an unnamed parameter — deliberately not printed, since a connection string can carry `PWD=` and the banner goes to CI logs — and the two `C4267` narrowings at `:46,47` are guarded `size_t` arithmetic. `/WX` is their regression test. The **duplicate storage** half is here: `all_results_` kept a full second copy of every `TestResult` (195 of them against the mock, each with five `std::string`s and two `optional`s) to build a severity-ranked list that is empty on a clean run. It is now `failures_`, filtered on the way in to just `FAIL`/`ERR`. **Test**: new `tests/unit/test_console_reporter.cpp`, 5 cases — a clean run prints no failure section; both `FAIL` and `ERR` reach the summary while `PASS`/`SKIP` do not; the summary is ordered CRITICAL → ERR → WARNING; failures accumulate across `report_category` calls; and a 200-character category name does not wrap the padding arithmetic (the C4267 site). The reporter takes an `std::ostream&`, so these assert on real rendered output rather than on internal state. | 31eb8a6 |

## Part H — Documentation and repo hygiene

| ID | Status | Sev | Area | Task | Details / Evidence | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | ✅ DONE | MED | Docs | Correct the mock pass-rate claim in `README.md` | **Redone a third time, because the number keeps moving and that is the actual problem.** It was `191/195 (97.9%)`; then 195/195; then **B1**/**B2** changed what the number *means* and it became 186/186 scored with 9 informational; since then **B2**'s reclassification, **I8** adding two probes and **C11** consolidating three away have moved it again. README now says **190/190 scored (100%)** with **15 informational** — 205 results in total — and the headline "195 tests" and the sample output's `Total Tests: 195` are corrected to 205, which the previous pass missed. **The informational split is verified, not asserted**: 6 record what the driver answered where the spec leaves no right answer (an optional attribute's value, a `SQLGetInfo` bitmask, the type `COUNT(*)` comes back as); the other 9 are driver-manager checks - `HY010` for execute-without-prepare, `24000` for no cursor, `07009` for column 0, `HY003`, `HY092` - which describe the stack rather than the driver. Both DMs enforce the state-transition table before dispatch, so a pass there is guaranteed and evidence of nothing. | 0ff22b8 |
| H2 | ✅ DONE | MED | Docs | Remove the "Firebird is not in the matrix" note | The note said Firebird was "intentionally deferred until the upstream driver refactor stabilizes" and that "the manifest schema reserves a slot". All three parts were wrong: `.github/drivers.json` has a full entry, `stress-test.yml` has a working job, and Firebird is the driver whose stress test has run *most recently*. Note removed; the driver table gains **Firebird ODBC Driver 3.5.0-rc1** and the count above it goes five → six. Worth saying: the run that closed **F1** had Firebird **segfaulting** in Descriptor Tests, which is exactly what a matrix is for. A driver kept out of the table on the grounds that it is not ready is a driver whose problems nobody sees. | 0ff22b8 |
| H3 | ✅ DONE | MED | Docs | Refresh `PROJECT_PLAN.md` | Version 3.1 → 3.2, "February 23, 2026" → today, 12 categories → **23**, "131 tests" → **205 probes**, Google Test 1.14/CLI11 2.4.2/nlohmann 3.11.3 → **1.17.0 / 2.6.2 / 3.12.0**. The figures are measured - `--list-categories` and a `Mode=Success` run - and the document now says so, because a number nobody can re-derive is a number that drifts again. **The mock schema section is the part that mattered.** It listed three tables; the mock has five. `USERS` and `ORDER_ITEMS` were missing, and **A24** records `catalog_depth_tests.cpp` believing this document rather than the driver. The columns are taken from `mock_catalog.cpp` - the code that answers `SQLTables`, and so the only version that can be right - not from either README. The "131/131 passing and 17 failed against a real driver" lesson keeps its point and loses its stale arithmetic, with **F1**'s fresh evidence attached: mock 190/190, PostgreSQL 87.1%, MySQL 57.7%, and the Firebird driver segfaulting. | 0ff22b8 |
| H4 | ✅ DONE | MED | Docs | Fix `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `AGENTS.md` drift | `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s tree listed `cli/`, deleted in `543ec85` - removed (`reporting/` was already listed, so the line simply goes). **`AGENTS.md` step 6 was worse than stale: following it produced nothing.** It said "add GTest unit tests in `tests/` and register in `tests/CMakeLists.txt`", referring to per-category wrappers retired in `cb2f639` - so a contributor adding a probe would write a test file with nothing to register it against. It now says to add an **e2e scenario with a mock configuration that makes the new probe fail**, which is the rule the rest of this plan has been enforcing: a probe with no failing configuration has not been shown to detect anything. `tests/unit/` is still named as the right place for a pure function. The include-order rule was settled under **C14**. | 0ff22b8 |
| H5 | ✅ DONE | MED | Legal | Add a `LICENSE` file | Added, MIT, at the repo root - `README.md` and `mock-driver/README.md` had both pointed at a file that did not exist, and the second says "See LICENSE file" outright. **The copyright holder is inferred and should be confirmed**: `F.D.Castel`, from `git log`'s author and the repository owner, with 2026 from the first and most recent commit dates. The licence *choice* was not mine to make - both READMEs already declared MIT - but the holder line is worth a glance from someone who can say so authoritatively. | 0ff22b8 |
| H6 | ✅ DONE | LOW | Hygiene | Delete or regenerate `register_mock_driver.reg` | **Regenerated as a template rather than deleted**, and the row offers both. The file hard-coded `C:\temp\new\odbc-crusher\mock-driver\build\Release\mockodbc.dll`, a path from a checkout that no longer exists; importing it registered a driver pointing at a missing DLL, and the failure surfaced later as a load error a long way from the cause. Nothing in the repo references it. It now leads with a header saying it will not work as-is and pointing at `register.ps1` (which **D30** hardened), and the path is an obvious placeholder. | I1 | ✅ DONE | MED | §8 | Diagnostic forwarding through unixODBC (5 FAIL + 3 SKIP) | Measured with **D1** on the green run at `437c402`: `[baseline] {'total_tests': 206, 'passed': 188, 'failed': 3, 'skipped': 0, 'errors': 0}` on `ubuntu-22.04`, against the locked baseline of **13 failed / 19 skipped**.  **Closed by D2, not by anything in this row.** The §8.1 hypothesis was that unixODBC pulls diagnostics via the legacy `SQLError`; Part 0 had already shown that could not be it, and the three code-verified causes ranked ahead of it. | I6 | ✅ DONE | HIGH | §3.8 | Per-connection isolation of inserted rows | **The title is wrong and the row should say so.** "Per-connection isolation of inserted rows" describes a per-connection *catalog*, and that would be a defect, not a fix: two connections to one database share **committed** data by definition. Modelling them as separate databases would break `CatalogSharing.SecondConnectKeepsTheFirstConnectionsTables`, which asserts exactly that and asserts it correctly. Even **D6**'s own note ("a genuinely per-connection catalog is the full fix") points at the wrong destination. What was missing is **transaction isolation**: a per-connection buffer of writes that have not landed yet. **Stage A** (`I6a`) built it. **Stage B** is this row: making it configurable, and giving the probe a configuration that fails it. **Two gaps had to be filled first, and both were silent.** `IsolationLevel=` **was never parsed** - `DriverConfig::isolation_level` existed and was assigned to `ConnectionHandle::txn_isolation_`, and no key ever set it, so it was always the default however the connection string was written. The same silent-no-op class **D26** fixed for `FailOn`. And `SQL_DEFAULT_TXN_ISOLATION` returned a hard-coded constant rather than what the connection was given. **The knob that matters is the one that lies.** An honest READ UNCOMMITTED **cannot fail an isolation probe**: a driver showing a dirty read at that level is behaving correctly, and a probe that failed it would be grading wrongly. So `DirtyReads=true` behaves as READ UNCOMMITTED while `SQL_DEFAULT_TXN_ISOLATION` keeps reporting READ COMMITTED - a real driver bug, and the only shape that can fail a probe of this kind. Reporting the truth there would remove the lie, so it deliberately does not. **The probe grades the claim, not the behaviour.** `test_uncommitted_row_isolation` reads `SQL_DEFAULT_TXN_ISOLATION` first, then opens a sibling connection (**C13**), holds a row uncommitted on it, and reads from the primary. A dirty read is **FAIL** when the driver promised READ COMMITTED and **INFORMATIONAL** when it promised READ UNCOMMITTED - recorded rather than graded, because there it is right. **Measured, all three**: default → `PASS`; `DirtyReads=true` → `FAIL`; `IsolationLevel=ReadUncommitted` → `INFORMATIONAL`. **Reference number moved, which the guardrail says to record**: **205 → 206**, scored and passed **190 → 191**, still 0 failed, 0 skipped, 100%. The normalised diff is exactly this one probe and the three summary counts and nothing else. `README.md`'s three figures are updated with it. **Tests**: mock 394 → **398** - `ReadUncommittedSeesAnotherConnectionsPendingRow`, `IsolationLevelIsParsedAndReported` (through `SQLGetInfo`, where an application would look), `DirtyReadsLiesAboutTheIsolationLevel`, and `DisconnectingRemovesTheBufferFromThePeerView` so a departed connection's writes stop being visible. Plus the e2e `IsolationProbeGradesTheClaimNotTheBehaviour`, which asserts all three outcomes - **AGENTS.md step 6's requirement**, and the sentence this row opened with turned into an assertion: an isolation probe now *has* a configuration that makes it fail. **Left as READ COMMITTED, said plainly rather than pretended**: REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE parse and are reported, and behave as READ COMMITTED. Modelling non-repeatable reads needs a per-transaction read snapshot, and `UPDATE` still does not apply its SET clause, which is a prerequisite. (**D83** has since applied it, so that half is met; the read snapshot is what remains.) | c9ee8eb |
| I6a | ✅ DONE | HIGH | Transactions | A per-connection buffer of uncommitted writes | **I6 Stage A.** The mock had **no uncommitted state at all**: every INSERT and DELETE landed in the process-global row store immediately, and ROLLBACK approximated "undo" by calling `clear_inserted_data()`, which deletes every row of every table **for every connection**. **The bug, written as a test first and watched to fail**: `RollbackDoesNotTouchAnotherConnectionsRows` - B commits a row, A rolls back something unrelated, B's row is gone. Two probes in the tool were reading a store this had already wiped: `connection_tests.cpp:279` and `transaction_tests.cpp:711` both do the sibling's rollback with autocommit OFF, so their "did the rolled-back row survive?" checks answered 0 whatever the driver did. **An ordered op log per connection, not a table snapshot.** Deleting a *committed* row needs to identify it and `MockRow` has no identity - no rowid, duplicates legal - so a `Delete{predicate}` op sidesteps that, and makes COMMIT a **merge** against whatever is committed at commit time rather than a replace. `CommitMergesRatherThanReplaces` pins that against a snapshot implementation. Storing the predicate is safe: `make_where_filter`'s lambdas capture by value. **Owned by `ConnectionHandle`**, not by a map inside `MockCatalog` keyed on a pointer - the handle gives correct lifetime for free, including the case `SQLDisconnect`'s 25000 creates where the connection stays open still holding uncommitted work (`RefusedDisconnectKeepsTheWriteSet`). **Two traps, both avoided deliberately.** The context is keyed on `autocommit_ == SQL_AUTOCOMMIT_OFF` and **not** on `in_transaction_`, which `statement_api.cpp` sets *after* the executor runs and so is still false during a transaction's first statement. And the autocommit OFF→ON path had to learn to **commit the buffer**, not just clear the flag - `RowsCommittedByRestoringAutocommitSurvive` would have broken silently otherwise. `apply_write_ops` takes the catalog mutex **once** and works inline; calling `insert_row` or `erase_matching_rows` would deadlock on a non-recursive `mu_`, which is the same reason `find_table_locked` exists. **What did not change, on purpose**: committed data is still shared between connections - two sessions on one database, which is what `CatalogSharing.SecondConnectKeepsTheFirstConnectionsTables` asserts and asserts correctly. DDL stays non-transactional. Stock-table materialisation stays committed-side: generated rows are not the transaction's work and a rollback must not un-materialise them. D28's `had_transaction` guard is gone because it is no longer needed - a rollback in autocommit mode now does nothing because the buffer is empty, rather than because of a special case. **Gate**: reference report **byte-identical** in both configurations - the reference run is all autocommit-ON work, so nothing moved. 118/118 unit, 43/43 e2e, mock 387 → **394**, 96/96 entry guards. **Verified**: 3 of the 7 new cases fail without the change. **A false regression I nearly recorded**: `AWedgedCrusherIsKilledRatherThanWaitedOn` failed three times running, and passed on a reverted build - but the cause was my own back-to-back runs. That test wedges a child with `Latency=30s`, so the orphan from the previous run is still holding its temp file for thirty seconds, and the assertion counts every `crusher_e2e_*` in the temp directory. With a 32-second gap it passes 3 for 3 with this change in place. | |
| I7 | ✅ DONE | LOW | PORT 3.E | `{?=CALL fn(?)}` return-value probe | **Done — same deliverable as A17**, whose row carries the full account. `test_function_call_escape_return_value` prepares, binds and *executes* `{?=CALL MOCK_FN(?, ?)}`, checks the function's return value arrives in parameter 1, and identifies the classic off-by-one (arguments bound from 1 instead of 2) with a confirmation call whose expected value under that misreading is exactly `0*10 + a`. Unblocked by **D13** (5), which taught the mock to parse the form and gave it a callable function to run against. Recorded on both rows rather than letting one silently cover the other. | 5cb4e51 |
| I8 | ✅ DONE | LOW | PORT 9.B / 10.B | Disconnect-with-open-transaction; pooled-connection recovery | Both implemented on **C13**'s sibling connection. **And both of my first drafts asserted things the ODBC spec does not say** - the guardrail asks which side is wrong when a probe fails against the mock, and it was mine, three times. **9.B.** I asserted `SQLDisconnect` rolls an open transaction back. The spec says the opposite is conformant: the driver returns **25000** and *the transaction is unchanged and the connection is open*. The mock does exactly that, deliberately, under **D28**. Rewritten to accept both conformant answers - refuse with 25000, or succeed having rolled back - and to fail only on the third: succeeding having **committed**, which turns a dropped connection into durable data nobody asked to keep. Then it failed again, because it read the row from a second connection while the first still had it open, which is a question about the *isolation level*, not about `SQLDisconnect` - at READ UNCOMMITTED the row is visible and nothing is wrong. It now ends the transaction first and asks afterwards. **Regression**: `DisconnectProbeCatchesTheWrongSqlstate` uses `FailOn=SQLDisconnect;ErrorCode=HY000` to make the disconnect fail with something that is not 25000, and checks the probe reports it and names the SQLSTATE it wanted. **10.B.** I asserted a reconnected handle comes back with autocommit ON. Connection attributes belong to the **handle**, not the session, and persist until changed or the handle is freed - a driver keeping the caller's setting is right. Rewritten to grade what is actually a defect: after a disconnect and reconnect the handle must be **usable** (execute and commit), and work the previous connection discarded must not reappear. The autocommit state is reported and explicitly **not** graded, with the reason in the message. **Regression**: `ReconnectProbeGradesUsabilityAndReportsAutocommit` asserts both halves are present. **No fault-injection lever reaches only its post-reconnect execute** - `FailOn` is global, so anything breaking the second statement breaks the first and the probe never gets there. Said plainly rather than left looking like coverage it is not. **Reference number moved 206 → 208, 191 → 193 passed**, which is these two probes being added and nothing else: still 0 failed, 0 skipped, 100%. Recorded because the guardrail says a moved number is a finding. | d7da92c |
| I9 | ✅ DONE | LOW | §6.5 | Revisit the partial `param_binding_tests.cpp` migration | Closed by **C1**, which finished the migration: all 14 hand-rolled bodies are on `run_test`, and the report was compared probe by probe before and after to show nothing else moved. The blocker this row inherited from prior §6.5 — per-early-return cleanup — was already gone when `RoundTripTableGuard` shipped, and **C4** has since made the guard the only implementation. | 93d31a9 |
