## Part J — Recommended execution order

All 120 tasks, sequenced. The order is driven by **dependencies and risk**, not by severity: several high-severity items are deliberately late because fixing them earlier would be unverifiable or would be undone by a later refactor.

Three principles shape it:

1. **Build the safety net before changing behaviour.** Right now the mock's own tests avoid nearly every broken surface, the Linux canary tolerates 29 non-passing probes, and the JSON output cannot be parsed by a machine. Change probe verdicts before fixing that and you have no way to tell a fix from a regression.
2. **Land the shared helper before the twenty call sites that need it.** Four findings (**A1**, **A2**, **A4**, **B3**) are each a one-line change repeated dozens of times. Doing them by hand first means doing them twice.
3. **Fix the mock before chasing the Linux gap.** Several §8 clusters are probably *symptoms* of mock defects found in this review (**D22**, **D24**, **D25**), not of a platform issue. Chasing them first risks building platform-specific workarounds for bugs that are not platform-specific.

**Do not start with**: the big refactors (**C8**, **C9**, **D18**, **D31**) — they are the right destination but they move code you have not yet made correct; the §8 Linux clusters (**I1**–**I5**); or implementing `SQLError`, which Part 0 shows is not the blocker.

Each phase below is independently shippable and leaves the tree green. Phase 8 is documentation and can be done by anyone at any time, in parallel with everything else.

---

### Phase 0 — Turn on the instruments *(no behaviour change)*

| Order | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **E1**, **D16** | Link the existing warning interface from all 8 targets; fix the 9 warnings it surfaces. `-Wshadow` alone catches **D14**/**D22**. |
| 2 | **D21** | Add `test_connection.cpp` to the mock build. Expect it to go red — that is the point. Whatever it reveals feeds Phase 5; record it here rather than fixing it now. |
| 3 | **G1**, **G4** | Progress text to stderr, `schema_version` + ISO-8601 in the report. Needed before any before/after report diffing. |
| 4 | **E2**, **E3** | `version.hpp` out of the source tree; one GoogleTest pin. Removes two sources of "why is my build stale" during the work ahead. |
| 5 | **F11** | Make the `.def` drift check bidirectional, so **D2** cannot regress once fixed. |

**Exit criteria**: warnings on everywhere and CI green; mock suite reports ~135 tests instead of 107; `odbc-crusher … -o json | jq .summary` works; two consecutive runs produce byte-comparable reports.

**Phase 0 result** — all met.

| Criterion | Measured |
|---|---|
| Warnings on everywhere | 8/8 targets link `project_warnings`; both projects rebuild clean under `/W4 /WX`. 14 warnings found and fixed, not the 9 predicted — see **E1**. |
| Mock suite grows | **107 → 133** (28 added, 2 of them `DISABLED_` pending **D32**). The plan's "~135" assumed all 28 would run. |
| `-o json \| jq` works | Verified end to end; previously failed with `Expecting value: line 1 column 1`. |
| Reports byte-comparable | Near enough to be useful: of 2,023 leaf values, 171 differ between back-to-back runs — 169 are `duration_us`/`timestamp`, and the remaining **2** are timings baked into `actual` by the cursor-stress probes. Recorded as **G6**. |
| Reference fixture | 195/195 on Windows throughout, unchanged by every commit in the phase. |
| Other suites | 27/27 unit, 14 → **16** e2e (two new scenarios for **G1**/**G4**). |

---

### Phase 1 — Stop losing results

| Order | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 6 | **A9** | `run_test` catches `std::exception`. Until this lands, any later fix that introduces a throw silently destroys all 195 results. |
| 7 | **D9** | Exception barrier at the mock's C ABI boundary + clamp `ResultSetSize`. Stops a malformed connection string terminating the host process. |
| 8 | **G5** | Fix the console-reporter warnings **E1** surfaced. |
| 9 | **F2** | A timed-out run still produces JSON. Same failure mode as **A9**, on the CI side. |

**Exit criteria**: no single probe, and no connection-string value, can destroy a run or the report.

**Phase 1 result** — all met.

| Criterion | Measured |
|---|---|
| A probe cannot destroy a run | `run_test` catches `std::exception` and `...`; proven by neutering the handlers and watching 7 of 9 new unit tests fail. |
| A connection-string value cannot kill the host | Every integer knob clamped at parse time, and all **96** mock entry points wrapped in a function-try-block that maps any escaping exception to `HY001`/`HY000` + `SQL_ERROR`. |
| A killed run still leaves a report | The JSON report is snapshotted to its file as categories complete, atomically, carrying `complete: false` until the final write. |
| Suites | mock 133 → **146**, unit 27 → **47**, e2e 16 (unchanged), 195/195 unchanged. |
| Cost | Snapshotting cost ~150 ms/run and took e2e 5.6s → 10.0s until rate-limited; now ~126 ms/run and e2e 5.4s. |

Two new CI guards were added alongside: `mock-driver/tools/check_entry_guards.py` (every entry point carries the barrier) runs beside the `.def` drift check.

---

### Phase 2 — Localized probe fixes *(no refactor)*

Each of these is a self-contained bug with an obvious correct answer. Do them in one pass; they are cheap and they immediately stop the tool lying.

| Order | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 10 | **D33**, **D34**, then **A5**, **A6**, **A7** | The three outright logic bugs: `strlen` UB on an unterminated buffer, `\|\|` where `&&` was meant, a comparison that was never written. **D33**/**D34** come first and were added during this phase: none of the three had any mock configuration that could drive its new FAIL branch, so the phase's own exit criterion was unmeetable without them. They are fixture work pulled forward from Phase 5. |
| 11 | **A3** | Clamp every driver-supplied length before using it as a buffer length. Handle `SQL_NO_TOTAL`. |
| 12 | **A8**, **A21** | Probes that FAIL correct drivers (**A8**) or PASS broken ones (**A21**). |
| 13 | **A11**, **D35** | `== SQL_SUCCESS` → `SQL_SUCCEEDED` across all 13 sites; distinguish "hit the row cap" from "reached the end". **D35** (added during this phase) gives the mock a way to warn mid-result-set, without which none of the 14 loops was testable. |
| 14 | **A12**, **G6** | Cursor-stress thresholds: nanosecond totals, an absolute floor, a trimmed statistic, and sample the diagnostic queue at the right moment. **G6** (added during Phase 0) rewrites the same two `actual` strings to stop baking raw timings into them. |
| 15 | **A10**, **A13**, **A25** | Width and spec-accuracy fixes: surrogate pairs on 4-byte `SQLWCHAR`, truncate-not-round in the NUMERIC probe, `SQL_DIAG_NUMBER` into an `SQLINTEGER`. |
| 16 | **A16**, **A18**, **A26** | Drain `SQLMoreResults` before reading output params; cap the catalog walks; accept `SQL_PARAM_TYPE_UNKNOWN`; the small-nits sweep (missing `<cmath>` first — it can silently disable every numeric comparison). **A17 moved to Phase 5**: it needs the mock to expose a callable function, which is **D13**'s work and **I7**'s existing blocker. |

**Exit criteria**: 195/195 still holds against the mock, and each fixed probe has a mock configuration that proves it can now fail.

**Phase 2 result** — 195/195 holds in both Debug and Release. The second criterion was met where it could be, and the gap is the phase's most useful finding.

| Criterion | Measured |
|---|---|
| 195/195 | Holds after every commit, verified in Debug **and** Release. Release was added to the routine after **A12** passed in Debug and broke all three CI platforms. |
| A failing configuration per probe | Achieved for **A5, A6, A7, A8, A11, A13** — but **only after adding four fixture capabilities**, because the mock could not fail any of them. That the reference fixture had no way to return a wrong number, an unterminated string, a per-row warning, or a declined optional attribute is why these probes could ship broken and nothing noticed. Recorded as **D33**, **D34**, **D35** and the `SupportsArrayBind` extension in **A8**. |
| Probes with no failing configuration | **A12**'s two threshold branches, **A16**'s deferred-writeback path, **A18**'s two new branches, and three of **A21**'s five sites. Each says so in its own Details, with the reason and what a future knob would have to do. None is left implied. |
| Deferred | **A17** only, behind **D13** — the mock cannot parse `{?=CALL}` and exposes no callable function. Moved to Phase 5 order 37 beside its blocker and **I7**. |
| Suites | mock 107 → **160**, unit 27 → **55**, e2e 14 → **22**. |

---

### Phase 3 — Build the shared helpers, then use them

This is the structural heart of the work. Each helper is written once and then applied; do not apply by hand first.

| Order | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 17 | **C2** | `TestBase::execute_first_working` — returns the winning query and records `(query, sqlstate, message)` for every failure. |
| 18 | **A2** | Route the 58 bare `SELECT`s through it. This is what makes the Escape Sequence, Numeric Struct and Cursor Stress categories work against Firebird. |
| 19 | **A1** | With the helper in place, a successful execute becomes conclusive: compare and FAIL instead of falling through to SKIP. Closes prior §2.8 properly. |
| 20 | **C5** → **B3** | Extract `first_sqlstate`, then `classify_failure`, then apply at the ~40 sites that currently choose SKIP without reading a SQLSTATE. |
| 21 | **A4** | Escape-sequence helpers return the SQLSTATE instead of `'NULL'`, and feed it to **B3**. |
| 22 | **C10** → **C4** | Make `RoundTripTableGuard` movable, then adopt it in the three files that reimplement it. |
| 23 | **A14**, **A15**, **A22**, **A19** | The transaction/cleanup fixes, done here because **C4** has just touched the same probes: autocommit save/restore, stale-table clearing, commit-rc capture, `verify_rows_persisted` hardening. **Run before order 22, not after** — these are independent correctness fixes, and landing them first keeps them out of the same commits as C4's delicate transaction-probe refactor. **A19 spawned three fixture rows**, two of them fixed in this phase because A19 was untestable otherwise: **D37** (`SQLGetData` never continued a long column) and **D39** (`register.ps1` registered a relative driver path, so every mock-backed unit test skipped silently); **D38** is filed and scheduled at order 40 |
| 24 | **C1** | Finish the `param_binding_tests.cpp` migration (closes **I9**). Its stated blocker no longer exists once **C4** lands. |
| 25 | **C3** | Delete the ~45 inner `try/catch (std::exception)` blocks made redundant by **A9**. |

**Exit criteria**: one fallback helper, one classification helper, one table guard, all used everywhere; `param_binding_tests.cpp` fully migrated; ~600 LoC removed.

---

### Phase 4 — Make the pass rate mean something

| Order | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 26 | **B2** | Add the reported-but-unscored status. Everything else in this phase depends on it. |
| 27 | **B1** | Fix or reclassify the ≥26 always-PASS probes. Most have an obvious missing assertion; the rest become informational. |
| 28 | **A23** | Reclassify the eight probes that measure the Driver Manager, or redesign them to reach the driver. |
| 29 | **C7** → **A24**, **A20** | Extract `discover_tables` and `get_string`, then remove the hardcoded `CUSTOMERS`, add the ClickHouse `ENGINE` variant and the Firebird-safe DECIMAL precision, and finish the round-trip matrix. |
| 30 | **B4**, **B6**, **B7** | Stop reporting infrastructure errors as unsupported features; report partial success; reconcile the contradictory verdicts and the wrong conformance tag. |
| 31 | **B8**, **B9**, **B10** | Text, naming and the error-clearing comparison. |

**Exit criteria**: every PASS in the report is a probe that could have failed; every SKIP names the SQLSTATE that justified it. The advertised pass rate changes — update `README.md` (**H1**) in the same commit.

---

### Phase 5 — Mock driver correctness

The mock is the reference fixture. Until it is right, "195/195" means little, and the Linux gap cannot be diagnosed.

| Order | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 32 | **D18** | The shared C-type descriptor table and the single "copy into caller buffer with truncation" helper. This one refactor is where **D7**, **D11**, **D23** and **D24** are actually fixed. |
| 33 | **D24**, **D23** | Post `01004` centrally; bound the `SQLGetInfoW` memcpy; widen the `SQLSMALLINT` length arithmetic. |
| 34 | **D7**, **D11** | Chunked `SQLGetData` (the `offset` parameter the new helper already takes); block-fetch attributes and the C-type gaps in the fetch path. |
| 35 | **D8**, **D15** | The three memory-safety paths (destructor iterator invalidation, implicit-descriptor double free, zero-length buffer write) and the `string_utils` narrowing plus the Windows-only wide-truncation divergence. |
| 36 | **D4**, **D5**, **D6**, **D20**, **D32** | Concurrency and shared state: the missing `SQLFreeStmt` lock, unlocked catalog readers, the connect-wipes-everything behaviour (closes **I6**), the shared RNG, and `SQLConnect`'s config clobbering + fault-injection blind spot (**D32**, added during Phase 0 by **D21**). |
| 37 | **D10**, **D47**, **D48**, **D13**, **D12**, then **A17** + **I7** | Parameter substitution and the off-by-N. **D47** and **D48** were found here and are done in the same block: the first is a Windows-only catalog-lifetime regression left by **D6** that was hiding 35 mock failures, the second is `SQL_NTS` being ignored on a bound parameter. Then **D12** parser robustness - the empty-string UB, partial numeric parses, and the unparsed-`WHERE`-deletes-everything fallback, which grew into a real predicate engine (AND/OR, grouping, IS NULL, NOT IN) so that reporting the gaps did not mean reporting the common cases. Once **D13** makes `{?=CALL fn(…)}` parse and the mock exposes a callable function, **A17** (execute the `{?=CALL}` form rather than only string-translating it) and **I7** unblock together. |
| 38 | **D22**, **D14**, **B11** | Populate the diagnostic header fields and remove the `row_count_` shadowing, then give the tool probes that read the three header fields it never touches (**B11**, found doing D22). Do this *before* Phase 6 — it likely accounts for three of the eight §8.1 probes on its own. |
| 39 | **D25**, **D28**, **D29** | `SQLProcedureColumns`' missing 11 columns and the catalog semantics; transaction state; the descriptor and attribute entry points that report success for unimplemented behaviour. |
| 40 | **D26**, **D27**, **D36**, **D38**, **D42**, **D43**, **D44**, **D46** | `FailOn` gating, the connection-string parser edge cases, widening fault injection past the 18-of-65 entry points it reaches today, the quote character the mock advertises but cannot parse, LIKE evaluation and derived tables (**D42**/**D43**), and then the three escape probes that depend on both (**D44**). (**D33**, **D34** and **D35**, which belong to this group, were done in Phases 2-3 — see order 10.) |
| 41 | **D17** | Grow the mock's tests alongside each fix above, not after. Start with the catalog column-count test that would have caught **D25**. |
| 42 | **D19**, **D31** | Object library for the tests; collapse the `unicode_wrappers` / `info_api` / `catalog_api` boilerplate (≈ −550 LoC). |

**Exit criteria**: mock suite substantially larger and green; 195/195 still holds on Windows; every fix above has a test that would catch its regression. **Met.** The mock suite went **107 → 372** and is green in Debug and Release. The reference run is **191 PASS + 15 INFORMATIONAL = 206, 100% of the 191 scored** — the *number* is not 195/195 any more and each move is recorded on the row that caused it: **B2** split scored from informational, **A17** and **B11** added four probes, **D44** moved three from informational to scored. What the criterion meant — no regression against the reference — holds throughout; the total moved only when a probe was added or started grading. Every fix has a regression test, with two limits stated on their own rows rather than glossed: **D5**'s test is an arrangement for the sanitiser rather than a deterministic failure, and the calls the Windows driver manager answers itself (**A23**'s eight, `SQL_DIAG_RETURNCODE`, `SQLSetCursorName`) cannot be made to fail from the driver at all.

---

### Phase 6 — Linux parity

**Run the two cheap experiments early** — during Phase 0 or 1, as a one-hour measurement — and record the numbers here. But do the *fixing* now, after Phase 5, because several clusters should have shrunk on their own.

| Order | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 43 | **D2**, **D3** | Export surface (`-fvisibility=hidden` + explicit list) and registration parity. Re-measure after each, separately, so you know which one moved the number. |
| 43a | **D52**, **D53**, **D54** | Fallout from **D2**, found the moment the `.so` stopped exporting its ANSI entry points and the managers started converting. D52 is the blocking one: without it a run against a driver that returns a byte which is not UTF-8 produces no report at all. |
| 43b | **D55**, **D56** | Found while diagnosing 43a rather than by looking for them: CI could stall for six hours on a hung child, and the two things that would have explained the macOS failure were both suppressed on failure. Both are prerequisites for orders 44-47, which are measurements read out of CI logs. |
| 43c | **D57** | The hang D55 made visible: crusher writes its whole report and then does not exit, under ASan, since D2. Diagnosis in flight - the run that answers it is the one whose `[D57]` lines say whether LeakSanitizer's at-exit scan is the wedge. |
| 43d | **D58**, **D59** | What D57's diagnostic actually found: the driver manager writes and reads past the buffer length the application declares, on both Linux and macOS, and three probes were handing it heap allocations with nothing to spare. D58 fixes the tool; D59 records what the macOS baseline has to say about the platform. |
| 43e | **D60** | The last of it: the discovery layer, which had the same bug in a worse form and no guard at all. Closes D57. |
| 43f | **D61** | And the five `SQLGetInfo` string paths in the probes, taken as a set rather than one per CI round - ASan halts at the first, so one at a time would have cost five runs to learn what one sweep does. |
| 43g | **D63** | The last ASan failure, and the instrument needed to see it: a category that faults vanishes from the report rather than failing in it, and every lookup assertion in the harness was silent about that. |
| 43h | **D64** | And the sweep done by enumeration rather than by grep, after the grep missed sites twice. Five more, then zero. |
| 44 | **D1** | Re-measure the Linux baseline. Compare against the pre-Phase-5 numbers. |
| 45 | **I1** | Whatever remains of the diagnostic cluster. If it is still non-zero, `ltrace` the `.so` to see which entry point unixODBC calls and when — that single observation discriminates between the registration and the clear-on-entry hypotheses. |
| 46 | **I5**, **I2** | The array-parameter cluster (8 SKIPs, likely one precondition — see **D11**) and the `SQLWCHAR` width audit. |
| 47 | **I3**, **I4** | Catalog patterns and cursor behaviour — check first whether **D25** and **D7** already closed them. |
| 48 | **B5** | Once the count reaches zero, delete the tolerance constants and restore the hard `failed == 0 && skipped == 0` contract prior §7.10 intended. |

**Exit criteria**: `kMaxFailed` and `kMaxSkipped` deleted from `test_e2e_scenarios.cpp`; Linux, Windows and macOS all report 195/195.

> **Finding against this criterion, recorded rather than lowered — D59.** The macOS half is unreachable by any change to this repository. Three probes fail there because the platform's driver manager writes a terminator one byte past the length the application declared, measured at buffer sizes 10, 3 and 1. They are true positives about the platform, not gaps in the mock or the probes, so there is nothing here to fix and `kMaxFailed = 3` is the honest number. Windows and Linux remain in scope for the criterion as written. (The 195 figure itself predates Phase 4's informational reclassification; the reference run is 191/191 on Windows.)

### Phase 7 — Remaining engineering

| Order | Task | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 49 | **G2**, **G3** | `--category` / `--filter` / `--list-tests`, then `--fail-on=<severity>`. Both make the tool adoptable by a driver project's own CI, and **G2** speeds up the e2e harness. |
| 50 | **C13** → **I8**, **D65** | Give `TestBase` the connection string; implement the two ports blocked on it. **I7** was already closed by **D13**. **D65** was found by I8's probes and had to be fixed for either of them to be verifiable at all. |
| 51 | **F1**, **F12** | Dispatch a full stress-test run and verify the apt pins that have never executed. **F11** was found by doing it: the run is green and the Firebird job inside it was killed at the timeout after the driver segfaulted. |
| 52 | **F3**, **F9** | Run the test suites in `release.yml`; exercise e2e in the Debug matrix slots. |
| 53 | **F4**, **F5**, **F6**, **F7**, **F8**, **F10** | CI hygiene, security and manifest cleanups: drop the leftover debug output, resolve `fetch-stress-test.ps1`, add `permissions:` blocks, stop the unpinned `curl \| bash`, fix the dead manifest URL, and the macOS `timeout` gap. |
| 54 | **D30**, **E4**, **E5** | Registration-script hardening; make the documented clang-tidy invocation actually work; decide whether the GCC/Clang warning branch gets `-Werror` (**E5**, added during Phase 0). |
| 55 | **C6**, **C11**, **C14** | Dead code, overlapping probes, and settle the include-order rule. |
| 56 | **C8**, **C9**, **C12** | The three big table-driven rewrites (≈ −950 LoC). Deliberately last: they are pure structure, and by now the code they move is correct and covered. **Outcome: the −950 estimate does not survive reading.** C8 measured −80 of ~300, C12 −37 of ~250; in both, most of the estimate assumed a uniformity the probes do not have. C9's largest item turned out to be a correctness defect rather than duplication → **D66**. |
| 57 | **D62**, **D67**, **D68**, **D69**, **D70** | The buffer sweep D62 deferred, now that it has evidence. **D67** first, because a leaking fixture makes every later measurement suspect. Then **D68** and **D69** - the twelve false positives and the twelve unbounded SQLSTATE reads the lever exposed. Then **D62**'s 44 buffers. **D70** is what the sequence taught: the crash guard overrides ASan's handlers, so none of those rounds could have produced a sanitizer diagnostic whatever the fix was. It paid for itself in one run: **D71**, the wild write inside unixODBC that four rounds of buffer fixes had been chasing without evidence. **D72** is the e2e assertion that crash was breaking; **D73** is why macOS was dying outright where Linux contained the same class of fault; **D74** is the diagnostic that finally said so (and the abort it introduced doing it); **D75** is the `SIGKILL` nothing can catch; **D76** is the guard-coverage gap found on the way. |

**Exit criteria**: no known correctness debt outstanding; the remaining backlog is documentation.

### Sequencing at a glance

| Phase | Tasks | Character | Risk |
|---|---:|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | Build and tooling, no behaviour change | Very low |
| 1 | 4 | Robustness | Low |
| 2 | 15 | Localized probe fixes | Low |
| 3 | 14 | Shared helpers + their call sites | Medium — largest diff, best covered by Phase 0's net |
| 4 | 12 | Result taxonomy | Medium — changes the advertised pass rate |
| 5 | 24 | Mock driver correctness | Medium-high — the mock is the fixture everything else trusts |
| 6 | 9 | Linux parity | Low once Phase 5 lands |
| 7 | 21 | CLI, CI, structural cleanup | Low |
| 8 | 10 | Documentation | None — parallelisable |
| — | 3 | **I6**, **I7**, **I9** — pointer entries, not independent work | I6 is closed by **D6**, I9 by **C1**, I7 stays ⏯️ DEFERRED |
| | **120** | | |

Every task in Parts A–I appears exactly once above; the count was checked mechanically against the task tables.

Per the maintenance rules at the top: one task per commit where practical, and update this file's Status and Commit columns in the same commit.

## Appendix — Reproducing the measurements

```bash
# Full local build + test (Windows, MSVC)
cmake -B mock-driver/build -S mock-driver && cmake --build mock-driver/build --config Debug -j 8
ctest --test-dir mock-driver/build -C Debug --output-on-failure          # 107/107
cmake -B build && cmake --build build --config Debug -j 8
ctest --test-dir build -C Debug --output-on-failure -L unit              # 27/27 (7 skip)
ctest --test-dir build -C Debug --output-on-failure -L e2e               # 14/14

# The reference fixture. Since B1/B2/A23/A20, A17, B11 and D44 this is
# 191 PASS + 15 INFORMATIONAL = 206 results, and 100% over the 191
# that are scored. A17 added one probe and B11 three; D44 moved three
# from INFORMATIONAL to scored without changing the total, by making
# them send the escape they are named for. A new probe, or a probe
# that starts grading, is the only thing that has moved these numbers.
# One of B11's remains informational - test_diagfield_return_code,
# because the Windows DM answers SQL_DIAG_RETURNCODE itself, measured
# against a driver that deliberately reports the wrong value.
# 9 of the informational probes have no right answer to grade (B2); the
# other 8 are answered by the driver manager before the driver is
# reached, so their PASS is not a fact about the driver (A23).
build/src/Debug/odbc-crusher.exe "Driver={Mock ODBC Driver};Mode=Success;Catalog=Default;ResultSetSize=10;" -o json -f report.json

# ...and again in Release. CI's e2e slots run Release, and it is materially
# faster: a timing-sensitive probe (A12) passed in Debug and broke every CI
# platform because the phase it measured fell below a threshold only in the
# optimised build. Register the Release mock first, then put Debug back.
cmake --build build --config Release -j 8 && cmake --build mock-driver/build --config Release -j 8
mock-driver/install/register.ps1 -DriverPath mock-driver/build/Release/mockodbc.dll
build/src/Release/odbc-crusher.exe "Driver={Mock ODBC Driver};Mode=Success;Catalog=Default;ResultSetSize=10;" -o json -f report-release.json
mock-driver/install/register.ps1 -DriverPath mock-driver/build/Debug/mockodbc.dll

# E1 / D16 — surface the warnings the current CMake configuration hides
cmake -B cmake-build-warn-app  -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS="/W4 /EHsc" && cmake --build cmake-build-warn-app  --config Debug -j 8
cmake -B cmake-build-warn-mock -S mock-driver -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS="/W4 /EHsc" && cmake --build cmake-build-warn-mock --config Debug -j 8

# D9 / F11 — source-level guards, runnable locally (both also run in CI)
python mock-driver/tools/check_entry_guards.py      # every entry point wrapped

# G1 — the documented pipe usage
build/src/Debug/odbc-crusher.exe "Driver={Mock ODBC Driver};Mode=Success;" -o json | python -c "import sys,json; json.load(sys.stdin)"

# A2 — NOTE: this command is obsolete since A2 landed. The bare literals are
# still in the source, but they are now arguments to
# TestBase::literal_select_variants(), which appends the FROM RDB$DATABASE and
# FROM DUAL forms. Grep for a bare `stmt.execute("SELECT ` instead — that is
# the shape that bypasses the helper.
# Original command, kept for the record:
cd src/tests && for f in *_tests.cpp; do
  bare=$(grep -oE '"SELECT [^"]*"' "$f" | grep -vi ' FROM ' | wc -l)
  fb=$(grep -c 'RDB\$DATABASE' "$f")
  [ "$bare" -gt 0 ] && [ "$fb" -eq 0 ] && printf "%-32s %s bare selects, 0 fallbacks\n" "$f" "$bare"
done

# C1 — run_test adoption per file
for f in *_tests.cpp; do printf "%-32s run_test=%-3s make_result=%-3s clock=%s\n" "$f" \
  "$(grep -c 'run_test(' $f)" "$(grep -c 'make_result(' $f)" "$(grep -c 'high_resolution_clock::now()' $f)"; done

# D2 — compare the Windows export list against what the sources define
grep -oE '^\s+SQL[A-Za-z0-9]+' mock-driver/src/mockodbc.def | tr -d ' \r' | sort -u > /tmp/exported.txt
grep -rhoE 'SQLRETURN\s+SQL_API\s+[A-Za-z0-9_]+\s*\(' mock-driver/src --include=*.cpp \
  | grep -oE 'SQL[A-Za-z0-9_]+' | grep -v '^SQL_API$' | sort -u > /tmp/defined.txt
comm -23 /tmp/defined.txt /tmp/exported.txt   # 38 ANSI entry points exported on Linux only
```
